𝐙 \mathbf{Z} -Categories I
Paul Lessard

Abstract
. This paper is the first in a series of two papers, 𝐙 \mathbf{Z} -Categories I and 𝐙 \mathbf{Z} -Categories II, which develop the notion of 𝐙 \mathbf{Z} -category, the natural bi-infinite analog to strict ω \omega -categories, and show that the ( ∞ , 1 ) \left(\infty,1\right) -category of spectra relates to the ( ∞ , 1 ) \left(\infty,1\right) -category of homotopy coherent 𝐙 \mathbf{Z} -categories as the pointed groupoids. In this work we provide a 2 2 -categorical treatment of the combinatorial spectra of [ Kan ] and argue that this description is a simplicial avatar of the abiding notion of homotopy coherent 𝐙 \mathbf{Z} -category. We then develop the theory of limits in the 2 2 -category of categories with arities of [ BergerMelliesWeber ] to provide a cellular category which is to 𝐙 \mathbf{Z} -categories as △ \triangle is to 1 1 -categories or Θ n \Theta_{n} is to n n -categories. In an appendix we provide a generalization of the spectrification functors of 20 th century stable homotopy theory in the language of category-weighted limits.



Preface

This paper is the first in a series of two papers,
\mathbf{Z}
-Categories
I and
\mathbf{Z}
-Categories II, which develop the notion of
\mathbf{Z}
-category,
the natural bi-infinite analog to strict
\omega
-categories, and
show that the
\left(\infty,1\right)
-category of spectra lives inside
the
\left(\infty,1\right)
-category of pointed homotopy coherent

\mathbf{Z}
-categories as the full sub-
\left(\infty,1\right)
-category
of
\mathbf{Z}
-groupoids. That theorem is carried out in the language of
Cisinski model categories.
1
1

1

See
[
Cisinski
]
or
\mathbf{Z}
-categories II Part II for a survey of
the relevant results translated from the French.
The work is split as it is for the first is a work of (nearly formal)

2
-category theory, and the second is almost entirely restricted
to homotopical concerns.

In a sense these two works aims to synthesize the 1963 combinatorial
model of spectra of
[
Kan
]
, with the 1983 homotopy hypothesis
of
[
Grothendieck
]
, by way of Australian style
2
-category
theory and the
2
-category of categories arities of
[
BergerMelliesWeber
]
,
in the context of the 2006 theory of Cisinski model categories of

[
Cisinski
]
interpreted as presentations of categories of homotopy
coherent models of limit theories.

The work is aimed to be readable both by topologists and category
theorists and, as such, many elementary notions which are well known
to one group, but much less well so to the other, are treated somewhat
explicitly. We beg the reader’s patience.

Introduction

We begin with a long(er) and semi-technical introduction. This introduction
should be read with the foreknowledge that all will be explained in
more detail in the body of the text.

Part I: A
2
-categorical treatment of Kan’s “semisimplicial spectra”

Kan’s Suspension

The work of
[
Kan
]
begins with a description of the reduced suspension
which is adapted to the geometry/combinatorics of the simplex category.
Kan describes, albeit not in these modern terms, the reduced suspension
of pointed simplicial sets as the left Kan extension

\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\Sigma_{K}=\mathsf{Lan}_{\text{\char 72\relax}_{+}}\left(\vbox{\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\nicefrac{{\triangle\left[n+1\right]_{+}}}{{\triangle\left[n\right]_{+}\vee\triangle\left[0\right]_{+}}}$
}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}\right)$$which assigns to every freely pointed $n$-simplex a ``fattened''
circle whose cross section shares the geometry of the $n$-simplex.
We will note that this functor is a quotient of (a post-composition)
of the endofunctor
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 16.21521pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.21521pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K:\triangle\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 86.1608pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 86.1608pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\triangle}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-8.77896pt\raise-7.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left[n\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.77896pt\raise-7.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.21521pt\raise-7.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 76.21521pt\raise-7.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left[n+1\right]}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$The implied factoring will become important when we understand categories
of sequential spectrum objects and $\Omega$-spectrum objects as particular
$2$-categorical limits.
\par\par\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{Sequential spectra and $\Omega$-spectra as oplax-limits and pseudo-limits
respectively}
\par Now, in a $1$-category there is a unique notion of the cone over
a diagram. However, in passing to $2$-categories, we immediately
come upon three further notions of cone over a $1$-diagram. Indeed,
in a $2$-category, we have in the strict cones an avatar of the $1$-categorical
notion of cone. But we also have pseudo-cones in which the role played
by identity $2$-cells in commuting triangles is instead played by
a coherent choice of invertible $2$-cells. More, we have and oplax
and lax cones wherein those $2$-cells remain coherent, i.e. functorial
in the 1-cells of the diagram, but for which the hypothesis of invertibility
is dropped (see Figures \ref{fig:strict-cone},\ref{fig:pseudo-cone},\ref{fig:oplax-cone},
and \ref{fig:lax-cone}). The oplax and lax conventions are then but
the two choices for the directions of the comparison $2$-cells. Importantly,
just as the data of a cone in a $1$-category constitutes a natural
transformation from a constant functor into the diagram, in a $2$-category,
the various notions of cones, pseudo-cones, oplax-cones, and lax-cones
constitute pseudo-natural transformations, oplax-natural transformations,
and lax-natural transformations from a constant functor into the diagram.

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
$$
}}}}}}\hss}\end{minipage}\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{4}}{lax cone}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[. ]{{Figure 4}}{lax cone}}}\end{minipage}\end{figure}\par Now, it is no big leap to go from Adams spectra to sequential spectrum
objects valued in a category $\mathsf{A}$ with respect to an adjunction
$L\dashv R$. Indeed a sequential spectrum object is comprised of
an $\mathbf{N}$-indexed set of objects of the category $\mathsf{A}$, $\left(X_{i}\right)_{i\in\mathbf{N}}$,
together with a set of maps $\left(X_{i}\xrightarrow{\varphi_{i}}R\left(X_{i+1}\right)\right)_{i\in\mathbf{N}}$.
As we'll see however, these are but objects of the oplax limit
$$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\left\{\cdots\xrightarrow{R}\mathsf{A}\xrightarrow{R}\mathsf{A}\right\}$$in the $2$-category $\text{$\mathsf{Cat}$}$ (or $\mathsf{CAT}$ etc.
depending on size) where the data of those structure maps $\left(\varphi_{i}\right)_{i\in\mathbf{N}}$
are the $2$-cells of the associated classified cone, and $\Omega$-spectrum
objects are but objects of the pseudo-limit over that same diagram.
Indeed, when the functor $R$ is an iso-fibration, the pseudo-limit
and conical limit coincide, whence $\Omega$-spectrum objects are
in fact but objects of the strict limit in all cases we concern ourselves
with here.
\par So called coordinate-free spectra (see \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{ElmendorfKrizMandellMay}{}{}]})
too admit this description after replacing the diagram
$$\cdots\xrightarrow{R}\mathsf{A}\xrightarrow{R}\mathsf{A}$$with the diagram
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern of sub-vector spaces of a particular choice of $\omega$-dimensional
vector space $\mathfrak{\mathscr{U}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{\mathbf{R}}^{\omega}$.
\par Returning to Kan's particular choice of suspension functor, and its
factoring we will get a natural transformation between right adjoint
functors
$$K_{\bullet}^{*}\Longrightarrow\Omega_{K}:\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}$$where $\Omega_{K}$ is the functor right adjoint to Kan's $\Sigma_{K}$.
These $2$-cells will then assemble to define an oplax natural transformation

functor between oplax limits
$$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\left\{\cdots\rightarrow\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\Omega_{K}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\Omega_{K}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\right\}\longrightarrow\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\left\{\cdots\rightarrow\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{K_{\bullet}^{*}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{K_{\bullet}^{*}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\right\}$$But since $K_{\bullet}^{*}$ is induced by an endomorphism of $\triangle$
we will show how may rewrite the category on the right-hand side in
a more elementary fashion.
\par\par\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{Factorization through $\left[\left(\_\right)_{\bullet}^{\protect\op},\protect\S_{\bullet}\right]_{\bullet}:\protect\Cat^{\protect\sf{coop}}\protect\longrightarrow\protect\CAT$
and the collage construction}
\par Indeed, we will observe that the diagram
$$\cdots\rightarrow\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{K_{\bullet}^{*}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{K_{\bullet}^{*}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}$$factors through the $2$-functor
$$\left[\left(\_\right)_{\bullet},\mathsf{Set}_{\bullet}\right]_{\bullet}:\mathsf{Cat}^{\mathsf{coop}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$$which takes a small category $\mathsf{A}$ and returns the large category
of $\mathsf{Set}_{\bullet}$-enriched functors $\widehat{\mathsf{A}}_{\bullet}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\left[\mathsf{A}_{\bullet}^{\mathsf{op}},\mathsf{Set}_{\bullet}\right]$
from $\mathsf{A}_{\bullet}^{\mathsf{op}}$ to $\mathsf{Set}_{\bullet}$ where $\mathsf{A}_{\bullet}$
is the free $\mathsf{Set}_{\bullet}$ enriched category on $\mathsf{A}$. That $2$-functor
however sends $2$-categorical colimits to $2$-categorical limits.
\par As such, we will find that we have equivalences
$$\hbox{\hskip $$\hbox{\hskip invoking the collage construction, it will be easy to describe
the oplax colimit
$$\underrightarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\left\{\cdots\leftarrow\triangle\xleftarrow{K}\triangle\xleftarrow{K}\triangle\right\}$$as the small category on the set of objects
$$\left\{\left.\left(\left[n\right],-k\right)\right|n\in\mathbf{N},-k\in\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}\right\}=\coprod_{-m\in\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}}\mathsf{Ob}\left(\triangle\right)$$with $\mathsf{Hom}$-sets defined by the expression
$$\triangle_{\mathsf{coll}}\left(\left(\left[n\right],-m\right),\left(\left[\ell\right],-\left(m+k\right)\right)\right)=\triangle\left(\left[n+k\right],\left[\ell\right]\right)$$with the composition laws coming from the right hand side of that
equality. The universal property of the strict colimit $\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\leftarrow\triangle\xleftarrow{K}\triangle\xleftarrow{K}\triangle\right\}$
is similarly enjoyed by $\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}$, the category with set of objects
$$\mathsf{Ob}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)=\left\{\left.\left[z\right]\right|z\in\mathbf{Z}\right\}$$with $\mathsf{Hom}$-sets generated by maps
$$\left\{\left.d^{i}:\left[m-1\right]\longrightarrow\left[m\right]\right|i\in\mathbf{N}\right\}$$and
$$\left\{\left.s^{j}:\left[m+1\right]\longrightarrow\left[m\right]\right|j\in\text{$\mathbf{N}\ $}\right\}$$subject to (un-bounded) simplicial identities. The obvious functor
$\triangle_{\mathsf{coll}}\longrightarrow\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}$ then induces an adjoint triple

the direction of the arrow)
$$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\left\{\cdots\rightarrow\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{K_{\bullet}^{*}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{K_{\bullet}^{*}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\right\}\longleftarrow\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\rightarrow\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{K_{\bullet}^{*}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{K_{\bullet}^{*}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\right\}$$More, the oplax limit
$$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\left\{\cdots\rightarrow\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\Omega_{K}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\Omega_{K}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\right\}\longrightarrow\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\left\{\cdots\rightarrow\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{K_{\bullet}^{*}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{K_{\bullet}^{*}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\right\}$$too arises as a pullback along a pointed functor
$$\triangle_{\mathsf{coll}\bullet}\longrightarrow\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\left\{\cdots\rightarrow\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\Omega_{K}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\Omega_{K}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\right\}$$which we will construct explicitly.
\par\par\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{Double duty and slick tricks}
\par\par Kan defines the category of semisimplicial spectra to be the full
subcategory of $\widehat{\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}}_{\bullet}$ subtended by colimits of
pointed presheaves of the form
$$\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}$$which is a stable $\left[z\right]$-simplex with something much like
the geometry of an $n$-simplex, as its set of stable $\left[z-1\right],\left[z-2\right],\dots,\left[z-n\right]$
simplices which are \emph{not }the basepoint mirror the combinatorics
of the usual $\left[n\right]$-simplex. We call this subcategory $\mathsf{LocFin}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$.
\par We will see that part of the adjoint triple induced by the functor
above factors through $\mathsf{LocFin}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$ as a co-reflective
subcategory of $\widehat{\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}}_{\bullet}$.
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern me may think as a $\left(z-n\right)$-sphere with an $\left[n\right]$-simplex
worth of nontrivial faces,
then we then have a sequence of adjunctions

642.1724pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 600.17241pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$where the rightmost adjunction is a reflective subcategory and the
left two adjunctions are co-reflective subcategories. As we will see
however in developing model category structures on these categories
the error becomes irrelevant as the data lost in the passage from
$\mathsf{LocFin}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$ to $\mathsf{LocSph}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$
are coherently contractible.
\par\par\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{Homotopy-coherent models of...?}
\par Kan goes on describe a horn-filling condition for mapping spaces of
pointed presheaves $\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}^{\mathsf{op}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Set}_{\bullet}$ in
$\mathsf{LocFin}\left(\widehat{\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}}_{\bullet}\right)$. In \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Brown}{}{}]} this is
replaced with an easier description. Brown defines the set of stable
horns
$$\text{$\underset{\mathsf{B}}{\Lambda}=$}\left\{\left.\underset{\mathsf{B}}{\Lambda^{i}}\left[n-k\right]\hookrightarrow S^{\left(-k\right)}\left[n\right]\right|n\in\mathbf{N},k\in\mathbf{N},0\leq i\leq k\right\}$$where the stable horns $\underset{\mathsf{B}}{\Lambda}^{i}\left[n-k\right]$
are defined much as the simplicial horns $\Lambda^{i}\left[n\right]$
of $\triangle\left[n\right]$ are. Indeed, in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Brown}{}{}]} it was proven
that this set comprises a generating set of acyclic cofibrations for
a (left transfer of) a pointed Cisinski model category
which was then proven, in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{BousfieldFriedlander}{}{}]}, to be Quillen
equivalent to the Bousfield-Friedlander model structure on sequential
spectra valued in pointed simplicial sets. The promised irrelevance
of the distinction between $\mathsf{LocFin}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$ and
$\mathsf{LocSph}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$ is that the adjunction between
them is a Quillen equivalence.
\par But Cisinski model categories are presentations of $\left(\infty,1\right)$-categories
of homotopy coherent models for limit theories. Consider, for example,
Joyal's model structure for quasi-categories. That model structure
is the minimal Cisinski model structure on the category of simplicial
sets in which the spine inclusions are trivial cofibrations, and the
generating set $\Lambda^{\infty}\left(\mathsf{V}\right)$ of anodyne extensions
for that model structure identify the fibrant objects as those which
are homotopy orthogonal to the spine inclusions. But strict orthogonality
of a simplicial set with respect to the set of spine inclusions is
equivalent to the so-called Segal condition on a simplicial set $X$,
$$X\left(\left[n\right]\right)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}X\left(\left[1\right]\right)\underset{X\left(\left[0\right]\right)}{\times}X\left(\left[1\right]\right)\underset{X\left(\left[0\right]\right)}{\times}\cdots\underset{X\left(\left[0\right]\right)}{\times}X\left(\left[1\right]\right)$$It is thus that we identify quasi-categories as providing a presentation
of the $\left(\infty,1\right)$-category of homotopy coherent $1$-categories.
\par Returning to the topic of Kan's model then one is wont to ask: what
essentially algebraic structure are Kan spectra to be understood as
homotopy coherent models of?
\par\par\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{A Globular perspective on Kan's suspension functor}
\par In a sense the purely simplicial geometry obscures something of Kan's
suspension. Quasi-categories are as economical as they are precisely
because the directed higher cells are obscure - everything is a composition
datum. Thinking globularly however, or for that matter complicially,
we see that since the simplex category $\triangle$ is a full subcategory
of the category $\mathsf{Cat}$ of small categories we may think of Kan's
suspension as taking each simplex, thought of as a $1$-category,
and returning the $2$-category with a single object and that original
simplex as its unique non-trivial $\mathsf{Hom}$-category. In this view,
Kan's suspension is but the simplicial avatar of the endofunctor
$$\Sigma_{\omega}:\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}_{\bullet}$$which takes an $\omega$-category and returns the $\omega$-category
with a single object and that original $\omega$-category as its unique
non-trivial $\mathsf{Hom}$-$\omega$-category. Letting $\Omega_{\omega}$
denote the functor right adjoint to $\Sigma_{\omega}$, it is clear
that the diagram which begat sequential spectra should then be understood
an avatar of the diagram
$$\cdots\rightarrow\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\Omega_{\omega}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\Omega_{\omega}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}_{\bullet}$$and the diagram
$$\cdots\rightarrow\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{K_{\bullet}^{*}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{K_{\bullet}^{*}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}$$should be considered as but the pointing of an avatar of the diagram
$$\cdots\rightarrow\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}$$where $S^{*}$ is the functor right adjoint to the functor $S:\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}$
which send an $\omega$-category $X$ to the $\omega$-category with
two objects $0$ and $1$ with $\mathsf{Hom}\left(0,1\right)=X$ and all other
$\mathsf{Hom}$-categories trivial.\par\par\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{$\protect\Z$-categories}
\par What then, we ask, is an object of the following limit?
$$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\rightarrow\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\right\}$$To answer this we consider that $\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}$ is an Eilenberg-Moore category
of algebras for a monad on the category of globular sets. Indeed we
recall that there exists a monad $T$ on globular sets and an isomorphism
$\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\widehat{\mathbf{G}}^{T}$. Then, just as with the functor
$$K^{*}:\widehat{\triangle}\longrightarrow\widehat{\triangle}$$the functor
$$S^{*}:\widehat{\mathbf{G}}\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{G}}$$which gave rise to
$$S^{*}:\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}$$is of the form $\left[S^{\mathsf{op}},\mathsf{Set}\right]$ where $S$:$\mathbf{G}\longrightarrow\mathbf{G}$
is the obvious endomorphism of the globe category which sends the
$n$-globe $\overline{n}$ to the $\left(n+1\right)$-globe $\overline{n+1}$.
\par The taking of the Eilenberg-Moore category for a monad $T$ is the
taking of a lax limit over the underlying endofunctor as a diagram
in $\mathsf{CAT}$. As such, since this operation commutes with limits, we
are granted the existence of an isomorphism
$$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\rightarrow\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\right\}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\hbox{\hskip 411.22394pt\hskip-47.26347pt\hbox{$\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\leftarrow\mathbf{G}\xleftarrow{S}\text{$\mathbf{G}$}\xleftarrow{S}\mathbf{G}\right\}$}\hskip-47.26347pt\hskip-411.22394pt\raisebox{2.54445pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.11778pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{677201}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94725pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.97221pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-411.22394pt\hskip 411.22394pt}^{\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}T}$$for a monad $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}T$. More, just as the universal property of the
colimit
$$\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\leftarrow\triangle\xleftarrow{K}\triangle\xleftarrow{K}\triangle\right\}$$is enjoyed by $\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}$ whose objects are in bijection with $\mathbf{Z}$,
with maps being generated by $\omega$-many face and degeneracy maps,
the universal property of
$$\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\leftarrow\mathbf{G}\xleftarrow{S}\text{$\mathbf{G}$}\xleftarrow{S}\mathbf{G}\right\}$$is enjoyed by the category $\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}$ of integer, as opposed to
natural number, indexed globes.
$$\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}=\left\langle\left.\vbox{\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 161.86458pt\raise-14.15277pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 183.75018pt\raise-4.33163pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 183.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\cdots}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}\right|\begin{array}[]{c}s\circ t=s\circ s\\
t\circ t=t\circ s\end{array}\right\rangle$$We refer to presheaves on $\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}$ as $\mathbf{Z}$-globular sets. Then,
to borrow a phrase from \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Weiner}{}{}]} (p. 52) :
\begin{quotation}``If this \emph{thing $\widehat{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}}^{\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}T}$} \sout{(sin)}
is to have a name, let that name be\emph{ $\mathbf{Z}$-categories} \sout{(simony
or sorcery)}.
\end{quotation}We'll denote the category of such by $\mathbf{Z}\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}$.
\par\par\@@unnumbered@section{section}{Part II: Limits in the $2$-category of categories with arities}
\par To complete the strict version of the story, we must identify a category
$\mathsf{A}$ and a regulus $\mathsf{R}$ in $\widehat{\mathsf{A}}$ which are to $\mathbf{Z}$-categories as the simplex
category $\triangle$ and the spine inclusions are to small $1$-categories.
To clarify precisely what this means and to find such a category and
regulus we appeal to Berger, Mellies and Weber's $2$-category of
categories with arities and Bourke and Garner's synthesis of this
with Day's notion of density presentation.
\par In \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{BergerMelliesWeber}{}{}]} those authors develop the $2$-category
of categories with arities $\mathsf{CwA}$ - a sage choice of a $2$-category
of small dense subcategories. That work then uses this $2$-category
to organize numerous characterizations of various algebras on presheaf
categories as instances of a more general theory. In particular, the
same apparatus is seen to characterize $1,2,\dots,n,\dots,\omega$-categories
as reflective subcategories of $\widehat{\triangle},\widehat{\Theta_{2}},\dots,\widehat{\Theta_{n}},\dots,\widehat{\Theta}$
respectively satisfying an ``abstract nerve criterion'', where the
$\Theta_{k}$ are subcategories of Joyal's category $\Theta$.
Bourke and Garner go on to note that in many cases, including those
just mentioned, by way of Day's notion of a density presentation,
we may in fact easily extract a regulus which carves out precisely
the subcategory of those presheaves satisfying the criterion of the
``abstract nerve theorem''. In the cases mentioned the canonical
regulus is the set of spine inclusions which, together with a little
slight of hand, implies that the inner horns carve out the same subcategories
(this recovers the earlier result of \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Berger1}{}{}]}).
\par What we do in this paper is to show that the obvious $2$-functor
$\mathsf{CwA}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}^{\mathbf{2}}$ which sends a full-and-faithful
right adjoint to the underlying functor reflects weighted limits.
From this it follows (see Example \ref{exa:A-cellular-description-of-Z-categories})
that
$$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\rightarrow\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\right\}$$is a reflective subcategory of $\hbox{\hskip 411.22394pt\hskip-46.75662pt\hbox{$\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\leftarrow\Theta\xleftarrow{S}\Theta\xleftarrow{S}\Theta\right\}$}\hskip-46.75662pt\hskip-411.22394pt\raisebox{2.54445pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.11778pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{677201}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94691pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94725pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.97221pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-411.22394pt\hskip 411.22394pt}$
and more that it is carved out by the regulus whose homotopical and
groupoidal analogue carved out Kan's semisimplicial spectra in $\widehat{\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}}_{\bullet}$.
\begin{rem}Since, in this work, we do not actually need to perform any explicit
computations with the categories
$$\Theta,\ \underrightarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\left\{\cdots\leftarrow\Theta\xleftarrow{S}\Theta\xleftarrow{S}\Theta\right\}\ \mathrm{or}\ \underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\leftarrow\Theta\xleftarrow{S}\Theta\xleftarrow{S}\Theta\right\}$$we leave explicit descriptions of those categories to the second
paper.
\end{rem}\par\par\par\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{Appendix A - Generalized spectrification}
\par All four $2$-categorical limits over $1$-diagrams are but instances
of the still more general enriched categorical notion of weighted
limits. If limits of diagrams of categories are compatible collections
of objects, then category-weighted limits are compatible collections
of diagrams valued in the constituent categories of a diagram of categories.
It is in this language that we identify the usual left adjoint to
the inclusion of $\Omega$-spectrum objects into sequential spectrum
objects as an instance of a rather more general reflection of weighted
limits over a $1$-diagram into conical limits over that same diagram.
\par\par\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{Appendix B - Oplax limits, oplax weights}
\par This second appendix explains why the weight $\left(\mathsf{J}\downarrow\_\right)^{\mathsf{op}}:\mathsf{J}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Cat}$
computes oplax limits as weighted limits. This is well known, but
not in general to topologists,
as such we include it for the author's edification and for the interested
reader's.
\par\par\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{Appendices C\&D - Technical lemmata}
\par In a third appendix we assemble some technical lemmata which would
clutter the exposition were they in the body of the text.

\par\par\@@numbered@section{part}{toc}{\label{part:A-2-Categorical-Treatment-of-Kans}A $2$-Categorical
Treatment of Kan's ``Semisimplicial Spectra''}
\par In this part we use some $2$-categorical notions to give a description
of the ``semisimplicial spectra'' of \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Kan}{}{}]}. Along the way,
in Remark \ref{rem:Chen-Kriz-Pultr-are-wrong}, we correct a subtle
error of \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Kan}{}{}]} and another related one found in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{ChenKrizPultr}{}{}]}.
\par\par\@@numbered@section{section}{toc}{Kan's Suspension: The Kan decalage and its associated suspension
and loop-space functors}
\begin{defn}Denote by $K$ the functor
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 16.21521pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.21521pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 86.1608pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 86.1608pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\triangle}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-8.77896pt\raise-7.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left[n\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.77896pt\raise-7.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.21521pt\raise-7.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 76.21521pt\raise-7.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left[n+1\right]}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-7.26707pt\raise-16.10263pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{d^{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.26707pt\raise-16.10263pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 86.33817pt\raise-16.10263pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 86.33817pt\raise-16.10263pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{d^{i}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-7.45053pt\raise-24.86346pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{s^{j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.45053pt\raise-24.86346pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 86.15471pt\raise-24.86346pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 86.15471pt\raise-24.86346pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{s^{j}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$and observe that there are natural transformations
$$\text{id}_{\triangle}\overset{\alpha}{\Longrightarrow}K\overset{\beta}{\Longleftarrow}\left[0\right]$$whose components at $\left[n\right]\in\text{$\triangle\ $}$
correspond to the maps $d^{n+1}:\left[n\right]\longrightarrow\left[n+1\right]$
and $\left\{n+1\right\}:\left[0\right]\longrightarrow\left[n+1\right]$
. These data comprise the {Kan decalage} (see \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{CisinskiMaltsiniotis}{}{}]})
and we illustrate them in Figure \ref{fig:The-Kan-decalage}. {Kan's
small suspension functor} and its associated loop-space functor (see
\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Kan}{}{}]}) are given by taking the quotient of $K$ by $\alpha$
and $\beta$.

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{5}}{The Kan decalage illustrated: the case
of $\left[2\right]$}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[. ]{{Figure 5}}{The Kan decalage illustrated: the case
of $\left[2\right]$}}}\end{figure}\end{defn}\par Let the functor $\Sigma_{K}:\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}$ be the
left Kan extension
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\Sigma_{K}:=\mathsf{Lan}_{\text{\char 72\relax}_{\bullet}}\left(\vbox{\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\nicefrac{{\triangle\left[n+1\right]_{+}}}{{\triangle\left[n\right]_{+}\vee\triangle\left[0\right]_{+}}}$
}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}}\right)$$with $\text{\char 72\relax}_{\bullet}$ being the pointed Yoneda embedding $\triangle_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\text{\char 72\relax}_{\bullet}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}$
and let $\Omega_{K}$ denote its right adjoint.
\par Now, as proven in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Kan}{}{}]}, we have homeomorphisms
$$\left|\Sigma_{K}X\right|\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\Sigma\left|X\right|$$for all pointed simplicial sets $X$ where $\left|\_\right|:\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Top}_{\bullet}$
is the usual geometric realization functor and $\Sigma$ is the usual
reduced suspension. More, the unit $\eta:\text{id}_{\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}}\Longrightarrow\Omega_{K}\Sigma_{k}$
of the adjunction $\Sigma_{K}\dashv\Omega_{K}$ is invertible
\begin{prop}(Propositon 3 \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{ChenKrizPultr}{}{}]}) The unit $\eta:\text{id}_{\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}}\Longrightarrow\Omega_{K}\Sigma_{k}$
of the adjunction $\Sigma_{K}\dashv\Omega_{K}$ is invertible.
\end{prop}\par\@proof The proof given in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{ChenKrizPultr}{}{}]} is correct modulo the detail
we'll explore carefully in Remark \ref{rem:Chen-Kriz-Pultr-are-wrong}.
\qed\end@proof
\begin{rem}The invertibility
of the unit is an important distinction between this adjunction and
the associated one in pointed spaces. For any set $E$ we have that
both the pointed simplicial set
$$\Sigma_{K}\left(\bigvee_{e\in E}\triangle\left[0\right]_{+}\right)$$and the pointed space
$$\Sigma\left(\mathsf{Disc}\left(E\right)_{+}\right)$$are wedges of $E$-many circles. In $\mathsf{Top}_{\bullet}$ however we may
compose these circles whereas in $\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}$ we cannot compose them
without first taking a fibrant replacement for $\Sigma_{K}\left(\bigvee_{e\in E}\triangle\left[0\right]_{+}\right)$
in the pointed Kan (a.k.a. test) model structure.
\end{rem}\par\begin{rem}In \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{ChenKrizPultr}{}{}]} we
find a functor $\Omega:\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}$ defined as
follows.
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\vbox{\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left\{\left.\left[n+1\right]\xrightarrow{x}X\right|d_{n+1}\left(x\right)=\bullet\right\}}$}}}}{\hbox{\kern-111.52216pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{ \left.\left[n+1\right]\xrightarrow{x}X\right|d_{n+1}\left(x\right)=\bullet}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}$$Those authors then go on to describe the functor left adjoint to $\Omega$
as follows.
\par Let $\triangle_{0}$ in $\triangle$ be wide subcategory thereof generated by the
objects and solid arrows of the following diagram (the dotted arrows
are present only to guide the eye, they are \emph{not} generators
of $\triangle_{0}$)
$$\vbox{\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern $$\immediate\immediate\immediate\vbox{\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left\{\bullet\right\}}$}}}}{\hbox{\kern-15.00005pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{ \bullet}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}}$$It's not hard to see that we have an natural isomorphism of functors

agrees with $\iota_{\bullet!}\Sigma_{0}\left(\triangle\left[n\right]_{+}\right)$.

the pointed simplicial set $X$ defined by the following push-out.

\par Set $\mathsf{X}:\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$ to be the diagram which
sends each $-n$ to $\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}$ and each $-\left(n+1\right)\longrightarrow-n$
to $\Omega_{K}$.
$$\cdots\xrightarrow{\Omega_{K}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\Omega_{K}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}$$As we stated in the introduction, $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\mathsf{X}$ is $\Omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Sp}\left(\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet},\Sigma_{K}\dashv\Omega_{K}\right)$
and $\underrightarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{X}$ is $\mathsf{Sp}\left(\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet},\Sigma_{K}\dashv\Omega_{K}\right)$,
the usual categories of $\Omega$-spectrum and spectrum objects respectively
(this is treated in detail in Example \ref{exa:Sequential-spectra-}).
Then, for familiar reasons (or alternatively our weighted limit treatment
of spectrification, Theorem \ref{thm: generalized-spectrification-strict-limit})
we get a spectrification adjunction

0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.20834pt\raise-2.06944pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 32.86111pt\raise-13.6111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\triangle_{\mathsf{X}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 11.33243pt\raise-3.5488pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$We recall that the category $\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}$ admits the pointed test model
structure Quillen equivalent to pointed spaces with the classical
model structure.{} Therefore, seeing as $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{X}$ is equivalent to the category
of sequential spectrum objects valued in $\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}$ with respect
to the adjunction $\Sigma_{K}\dashv\Omega_{K}$, we may equip $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{X}$
with \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Hovey}{}{}]}'s stable model structure.
\par Then, since we have homeomorphisms $\left|\Sigma_{K}X\right|\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\Sigma\left|X\right|$
for all pointed simplicial sets $X$, it follows that Hovey's stable
model structure on $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{X}$ is Quillen equivalent to the
Bousfield-Friedlander model structure on sequential spectra, whence
it presents the $\left(\infty,1\right)$-category of spectra. Kan's
observation, as we will elaborate below in the next section, is that
by having chosen a particular presentation of the suspension, i.e.
$\Sigma_{K}$, the category $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{X}$ admits an elegant description
as a subcategory of a category of pointed presheaves.

The categories $\underrightarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{\mathsf{K}}$ and $\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\mathsf{\mathsf{K}}$
admit explicit descriptions. Recall that the collage construction
presents the oplax colimits of an arrow in $\mathsf{Prof}$. Iterating this
construction gives us a presentation of the category $\underrightarrow{\mathsf{oplax}}\mathsf{K}$.
Indeed, set $\triangle_{\mathsf{coll}}$ to be the category generated by
$$\coprod_{-m\in\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}}\triangle$$together with
$$\triangle_{\mathsf{coll}}\left(\left(\left[n\right],-m\right),\left(\left[\ell\right],-\left(m+k\right)\right)\right)=\triangle\left(\left[n+k\right],\left[\ell\right]\right)$$and see that $\triangle_{\mathsf{coll}}$ enjoys the universal property of
$\underrightarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{K}$.

\end{rem}\par The universal property of the strict colimit $\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\mathsf{K}$ is similarly
enjoyed by $\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}$, the category whose objects are in bijection
with the set $\mathbf{Z}$,
$$\mathsf{Ob}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)=\left\{\left.\left[z\right]\right|z\in\mathbf{Z}\right\}$$with $\mathsf{Hom}$-sets generated by maps
$$\left\{\left.d^{i}:\left[m-1\right]\longrightarrow\left[m\right]\right|i\in\mathbf{N}\right\}$$and
$$\left\{\left.s^{j}:\left[m+1\right]\longrightarrow\left[m\right]\right|j\in\text{$\mathbf{N}\ $}\right\}$$subject to (un-bounded) simplicial identities. One gets to the category
$\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\mathsf{K}$ from the category $\underrightarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{K}$
by identifying objects along the diagonal maps of $\triangle_{\mathsf{coll}}$.
Indeed, this defines a functor $\rho:\triangle_{\mathsf{coll}}\longrightarrow\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}$,
whence an adjoint triple

14.38123pt\raise-43.89432pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.17209pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{K_{\bullet}^{*}}$}}}\kern 38.0764pt\raise-37.72223pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$These squares can be pasted together providing an oplax natural transformation
$\mathsf{X}\xRightarrow{\mathsf{oplax}}\widehat{\mathsf{K}}$, whence we have a functor
$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{X}\longrightarrow\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\widehat{\mathsf{K}}$. But, since $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{\widehat{K}}$
is a category of pointed presheaves, this map admits a more explicit
description. Indeed that functor is a restricted Yoneda embedding,
or nerve.

\par These pseudo-cones define maps
$$\Phi^{\left[-n,\infty\right)}:\triangle_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\underleftarrow{\mathsf{pslim}}\mathsf{X}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\mathsf{X}$$\par\begin{rem}In the perhaps more familiar notation of sequential spectrum objects
the maps are described by the formula
$$\left\{\Phi^{\left[-n,\infty\right)}\left(\triangle\left[r\right]\right)_{-i}=\begin{cases}\bullet&i<n\\
\Sigma_{K}^{\left(i-n\right)}\triangle\left[r\right]_{+}&i\geq n\end{cases}\right\}$$\end{rem}\par We observe then that the diagrams

$$\Phi:\underrightarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{K}\longrightarrow\underleftarrow{\mathsf{pslim}}\mathsf{X}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\mathsf{X}$$We may of course compose $\Phi$ with the reflective subcategory
$$\mathsf{sp_{\mathsf{X}}}\dashv\triangle_{\mathsf{X}}:\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\mathsf{X}\leftrightarrows\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{X}$$That composition $\triangle_{\mathsf{X}}\Phi:\underrightarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{K}\longrightarrow\underrightarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{X}$
induces an adjoint triple.

above with the adjunction $\rho_{\bullet!}\dashv\rho_{\bullet}^{*}$
provides a third adjunction

\par We will be concerned with two interesting and important subcategories
of $\widehat{\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}}_{\bullet}$. Both are full subcategories on colimits of objects
of specific forms.
\begin{defn}For each $n\in\mathbf{N}$ and $z\in\mathbf{Z}$, define the pointed presheaf $\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}$
by the following pushout in $\widehat{\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}}_{\bullet}$.

\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 10.18231pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\line@@}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lower-5.0pt\hbox{\line@@}}}\hbox{}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$Similarly, for each $n\in\mathbf{N}$ and $z\in\mathbf{Z}$, let
$$S^{\left(z-n\right)}\left[n\right]=\nicefrac{{\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\left[z\right]}}{{d^{n}d^{n-1}\cdots d^{1}d^{0}=d^{>n}=*}}$$be defined by the following pushout in $\widehat{\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}}_{\bullet}$.
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 201.61307pt\raise-44.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 201.61307pt\raise-44.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces S^{\left(z-n\right)}\left[n\right]}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 212.81161pt\raise-44.68945pt\hbox{\ignorespaces
\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 10.18231pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\line@@}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lower-5.0pt\hbox{\line@@}}}\hbox{}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$We define $\mathsf{LocFin}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$ to be the full subcategory
of $\widehat{\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}}_{\bullet}$ on objects which are colimits of objects of the
form $\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}$ and we define $\mathsf{LocSph}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$
to be the full subcategory of $\widehat{\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}}_{\bullet}$ on colimits of objects
of the form $S^{\left(z-n\right)}\left[n\right]$.
\end{defn}\par\begin{rem}As we discussed in the introduction, the pointed presheaf $\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}$
is a stable $\left[z\right]$-simplex with precisely $n$-many codimension
1 faces and the pointed presheaf $S^{\left(z-n\right)}\left[n\right]$
is a stable $\left[z\right]$-simplex which is, in essence, an $\left[n\right]$-simplex
worth of $\left(z-n\right)$-spheres.
\end{rem}\par It is immediate, as observed in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{ChenKrizPultr}{}{}]}, that $\mathsf{LocFin}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$
is a coreflective subcategory of $\widehat{\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}}_{\bullet}$ with the right
adjoint functor picking out, for each presheaf, the sub-object of
cells of the form $\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}$ . The same
argument provides that $\mathsf{LocSph}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$ is coreflective
in $\mathsf{LocFin}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$.

0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 533.2893pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces)@{{}^{(}->}[ll]\mathsf{LocSph}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 606.15192pt\raise-7.99942pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$\par Now, as we will show, every presheaf $Z:\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}^{\mathsf{op}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Set}_{\bullet}$
which is $\rho_{\bullet!}\circ\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{X}}\Phi\right)_{\bullet}^{*}$
of something satisfies an important and interesting property - it
is a colimit of pointed presheaves $S^{\left(z-n\right)}\left[n\right]$.
\par Every object of the category $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{X}$ is canonically the
colimit of objects of the form
$$\mathsf{in}_{\mathsf{X}}\Phi\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{coll}}\left(\left[n\right],-k\right)\right)$$as any simplex in any index gives rise to such. It then follows that,
since $\rho_{\bullet!}\circ\left(\mathsf{in}_{\mathsf{X}}\Phi\right)_{\bullet}^{*}$
preserves colimits, every $Z:\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}^{\mathsf{op}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Set}_{\bullet}$
which is $\rho_{\bullet!}\circ\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{X}}\Phi\right)_{\bullet}^{*}$
of something is a colimit of objects of the form
$$\rho_{\bullet!}\circ\left(\mathsf{in}_{\mathsf{X}}\Phi\right)_{\bullet}^{*}\circ\mathsf{in}_{\mathsf{X}}\Phi\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{coll}}\left(\left[n\right],-k\right)\right)$$As it turns out however, these are precisely the stable simplicial
sets $S^{\left(z-n\right)_{\left[n\right]}}$.
\begin{lem}For all $n\in\text{$\mathbf{N}$}$ and $k\in\mathbf{N}$,
$$\rho_{\bullet!}\circ\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{X}}\Phi\right)_{\bullet}^{*}\circ\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{X}}\Phi\right)\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{coll}}\left(\left[n\right],-k\right)\right)=S^{-k}\left[n\right]$$\end{lem}\par\@proof Consider the object $\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{X}}\Phi\right)_{\bullet}^{*}\circ\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{X}}\Phi\right)\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{coll}}\left(\left[n\right],-k\right)\right)$
in the sequential notation.
$$\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{X}}\Phi\right)_{\bullet}^{*}\circ\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{X}}\Phi\right)\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{coll}}\left(\left[n\right],-k\right)\right)_{-\ell}=\begin{cases}\bullet&\ell<k\\
\Sigma_{K}^{\left(\ell-k\right)}\triangle\left[n\right]_{+}&\ell\geq k\end{cases}$$\qed\end@proof
We've thus the promised (in the introduction) composition of adjunctions
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\hbox{\hskip 411.22394pt\hskip-7.3pt\hbox{$\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}$}\hskip-7.3pt\hskip-411.22394pt\raisebox{1.98889pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.11778pt}{\resizebox{48365776}{677201}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 0.1pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern 0.1pt\mathchar 866\kern 132.42093pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathsf{KSp}}$}}}\kern isomorphism, whence asserting the right adjoint functor to be full-and-faithful.
It's not hard to show however that $\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}$
is in $\mathsf{LocFin}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$ but \emph{not} in $\mathsf{LocSph}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$,
so the component of co-unit of the adjunction at the object $\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}$
is \emph{not }the identity.
\end{example}\par The right hand adjunction however is, by Kan's observation, a reflective
subcategory.
\begin{lem}The co-unit of the adjunction
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 29.70282pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-29.70282pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{LocSph}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.50975pt\raise-17.43054pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathsf{KPs}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 71.94714pt\raise-5.55412pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 47.70282pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 71.70282pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.33473pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathsf{KSp}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 35.39308pt\raise-5.43054pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bot}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.70284pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$is a natural isomorphism, whence $\mathsf{LocSph}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)\longrightarrow\underrightarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{X}$
is full-and-faithful.
\end{lem}\par\@proof This can be shown by direct computation. Suppose $Z:\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}^{\mathsf{op}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Set}_{\bullet}$
to be of $\mathsf{LocSph}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$. Then the pointed simplicial
sets $\mathsf{KPs}\left(Z\right)_{-i}$ may be described as follows
$$\vbox{\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 33.28549pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-33.28549pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{KPs}\left(Z\right)_{-i}:\triangle^{\mathsf{op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.28465pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 69.28465pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{Set}_{\bullet}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-8.77896pt\raise-8.38042pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left[n\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.77896pt\raise-8.38042pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.28549pt\raise-8.38042pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 51.28549pt\raise-8.38042pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widehat{\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}}_{\bullet}\left(S^{-i}\left[n\right],Z\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}$$so it is clear enough that the co-unit is a natural isomorphism.
\qed\end@proof
\begin{rem}For the above to really be as clear as we assert, it is important
to note that there are quotient relationships between the spheres
$S^{-k}\left[n\right]$. For example $S^{1}\left[1\right]\longrightarrow S^{2}\left[0\right]$
is a quotient.
\end{rem}\par As it turns out however, in the next section, we'll show that this
subtle error of Kan's, repeated in a slightly different guise in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{ChenKrizPultr}{}{}]},
ends up irrelevant once we go from categories to model categories.
\par\par\@@numbered@section{section}{toc}{Homotopy-coherent models of...?}
\par In 1973 \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Brown}{}{}]} synthesized Kan's treatment of spectra with
the then new notion of model categories (really with the notion of
categories of fibrant objects also developed in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Brown}{}{}]}).
\begin{defn}( \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Brown}{}{}]} ) Let $z\in\mathbf{Z}$, $n\in\mathbf{N}$ and $0\leq i\leq n$ be
given and set
$$\underset{\mathsf{B}}{\Lambda}^{i}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}\hookrightarrow\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}$$to be the union, in $\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>m}=*}}$, of the
images of the set of simplices
$$\left\{d^{k}:\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\left[z-1\right]\longrightarrow\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}\right\}_{k\in\mathbf{N}-\left\{i\right\}}$$Similarly, let
$$\partial\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}\hookrightarrow\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}$$be the union of the images of the set of simplices
$$\left\{d^{k}:\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\left[z-1\right]\longrightarrow\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}\right\}_{k\in\mathbf{N}}$$\end{defn}\par\begin{thm}(\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Brown}{}{}]}) The sets
$$\Lambda_{\mathsf{Brown}}=\left\{\left.\underset{\mathsf{B}}{\Lambda}^{i}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}\hookrightarrow\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}\right|z\in\mathbf{Z},n\in\mathbf{N},0\leq i\leq n\right\}$$and
$$\partial_{\mathsf{Brown}}=\left\{\left.\partial\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}\hookrightarrow\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}\right|z\in\mathbf{Z},n\in\mathbf{N}\right\}$$comprise a set of generating acyclic cofibrations and generating co-fibration
for a model structure on $\mathsf{LocFin}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$. More:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@This model structure is the left transfer of a pointed Cisinski model
structure on $\widehat{\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}}_{\bullet}$; and
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@The adjunction
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 28.38338pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.38338pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{LocFin}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.85002pt\raise-17.43054pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathsf{KPs}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 70.80391pt\raise-5.55412pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 46.38338pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 70.38338pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern \end{itemize}\end{thm}\par\@proof See Theorem 5 and Proposition 7 of \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Brown}{}{}]}.
\qed\end@proof
This model structure can further be left transported to $\mathsf{LocSph}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$.

\end{cor}\par\@proof To prove that the model structure generated by $\Lambda_{\mathsf{Spherical}}$
and $\partial_{\mathsf{Spherical}}$ coincides with the left transferred
model structure is to prove, for $f:X\longrightarrow Y$ in $\mathsf{LocSph}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$,
that $f\in\mathsf{Cell}\left(\Lambda_{\mathsf{Spherical}}\right)$ if and
only if $f\in\mathsf{Cell}\left(\Lambda_{\mathsf{Brown}}\right)$ and likewise
for $\partial_{\mathsf{Spherical}}$ and $\partial_{\mathsf{Brown}}$.
\par Since the inclusion functor is left adjoint, and both $\Lambda_{\mathsf{Spherical}}$
and $\partial_{\mathsf{Spherical}}$ lie in $\mathsf{Cell}\left(\Lambda_{\mathsf{Brown}}\right)$
and $\mathsf{Cell}\left(\partial_{\mathsf{Brown}}\right)$ respectively, the
``if direction'' is clear. Conversely, since membership in $\mathsf{LocSph}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$
is defined simplex-wise it follows by a simple factorization argument
that any $f$ from $\text{$\mathsf{LocSph}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$}$
which lies in $\mathsf{Cell}\left(\Lambda_{\mathsf{Brown}}\right)$ and $\mathsf{Cell}\left(\partial_{\mathsf{Brown}}\right)$
actually lies in $\mathsf{Cell}\left(\Lambda_{\mathsf{Spherical}}\right)$ or
$\mathsf{Cell}\left(\partial_{\mathsf{Spherical}}\right)$ respectively. Indeed,
it's clear that we have factorizations as below.

- consider that
$$\pi_{z}\left(\_\right)=\left[S^{z}\left[0\right],\_\right]_{\bullet}$$\qed\end@proof
Having found model categories for spectra which admit interpretation
as homotopy coherent models of a limit theory, both the model structure
on $\mathsf{LocSph}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$ and $\mathsf{LocFin}\left(\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\right)$
permit this, we are wont to ask - what is that limit theory a description
of?
\par\par\@@numbered@section{section}{toc}{A Globular Perspective on Kan's Suspension Functor}
\par Kan's model structure is intuitively interpreted as describing homotopy
coherent groupoids. However, in this interpretation one usually thinks
of every simplex merely as composition datum - unless one is accustomed
to thinks in terms of higher categorical nerves and oriented simplices.
As such it may not be clear how to relate the notions of composition
embodied by lifting against
$$\underset{\mathsf{S}}{\Lambda}^{i}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}\longrightarrow\triangle_{\mathsf{st}}\nicefrac{{\left[z\right]}}{{d^{>n}=*}}$$for different values of $z$ and $n$. We contend that making the
globular content more explicit makes things a bit more clear.
\par Since the simplex category $\triangle$ is a full subcategory of the category
$\mathsf{Cat}$ of small categories we may think of Kan's suspension as taking
each simplex, thought of as a $1$-category, and returning the $2$-category
with a single object and that original simplex as its unique $\mathsf{Hom}$-category.
In this view, Kan's suspension is but the simplicial avatar of the
endo-functor
$$\Sigma_{\omega}:\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}_{\bullet}$$which takes an $\omega$-category and returns the $\omega$-category
with a single object and that original $\omega$-category as its unique
$\mathsf{Hom}$-$\omega$-category.
\begin{rem}Leaving simplicial sets and going to globular ones is not of course
the only way to see this. Indeed we could, and probably should, think
of Kan's suspension $\Sigma_{K}$ as an endo-functor on strict $\omega$-categories
as complicial sets, which sends an $\left[n\right]$-simplex to a
thick $\left[n+1\right]$-simplex $\Sigma_{K}\left[n+1\right]_{+}$.
Had this author known of that material prior to developing this work,
he might well have gone in that direction instead.
\end{rem}\par\begin{rem}Importantly, this perspective is compatible with the interpretation
of Kan's model structure as providing a description of homotopy coherent
$\omega$-groupoids, as a composition datum for $n$-many $1$-cells
passes to composition datum for $n$-many $2$-cells.
\par Let $\Omega_{\omega}$ denote the functor right adjoint to $\Sigma_{\omega}$.
The diagram which begat sequential spectra should then be understood
an avatar of the diagram
$$\cdots\rightarrow\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\Omega_{\omega}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{\Omega_{\omega}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}_{\bullet}$$and the diagram
$$\cdots\rightarrow\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{K_{\bullet}^{*}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}\xrightarrow{K_{\bullet}^{*}}\widehat{\triangle}_{\bullet}$$should be considered as but the pointing of an avatar of the diagram
$$\cdots\rightarrow\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}$$where $S^{*}$ is the functor right adjoint to the functor $S:\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}$
which sends an $\omega$-category $X$ to the $\omega$-category with
two objects $0$ and $1$ with $\mathsf{Hom}\left(0,1\right)=X$ and all other
$\mathsf{Hom}$-categories empty.
\end{rem}\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{section}{toc}{$\protect\Z$-categories}
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{Strict $\omega$-categories and Berger's Cellular Nerve}
\begin{defn}Let $\mathbf{G}$ denote the{ globe category}, the category
$$\left\langle\left.\vbox{\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 4.33163pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
