0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 66.61458pt\raise-14.15277pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.15279pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 88.50018pt\raise-4.33163pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 88.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\cdots}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}\right|\begin{array}[]{c}s\circ t=s\circ s\\
t\circ t=t\circ s\end{array}\right\rangle$$Let the category of {globular sets }be the presheaf category
$\widehat{\mathbf{G}}$ and let $T:\widehat{\mathbf{G}}\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{G}}$ be the monad
whose algebras are strict-$\omega$-categories.
\end{defn}\par It was first proved in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Berger1}{}{}]} that strict $\omega$-categories
are a reflective, indeed orthogonal, subcategory of the category of
cellular sets $\widehat{\Theta}$. We repeat the treatment of the category
$\Theta$ developed first in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Berger2}{}{}]}, however our presentation
borrows more from \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{CisinskiMaltsiniotis}{}{}]}.
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsubsection}{toc}{Segal's category $\Gamma$}
\par Segal's category $\Gamma$ is a skeleton - in the sense of a small
category of chosen representatives for each isomorphism class - for
the opposite category of the category of finite pointed sets.
\begin{defn}Let $\Gamma$, {Segal's gamma category}, be the category specified
thus: let
$$\mathsf{Ob}\left(\Gamma\right)=\left\{\left.\left\langle k\right\rangle=\left\{1,\dots,k\right\}\right|k\geq 1\right\}\cup\left\{\left\langle 0\right\rangle=\varnothing\right\},$$and let $\Gamma\left(\left\langle n\right\rangle,\left\langle m\right\rangle\right)$
be defined by the expression
$$\Gamma\left(\left\langle n\right\rangle,\left\langle m\right\rangle\right)=\left\{\left.\varphi:\left\langle n\right\rangle\longrightarrow\mathsf{Sub}_{\mathsf{Set}}\left(\left\langle m\right\rangle\right)\right|\forall i\neq j\in\left\langle m\right\rangle,\ \varphi\left(i\right)\cap\varphi\left(j\right)=\varnothing\right\}$$where, for any category $A$ and object $a$ thereof, $\mathsf{Sub}_{A}\left(a\right)$
is the category of subobjects of $a$. Define the composition of morphisms
in $\Gamma$ by setting
$$\left\langle\ell\right\rangle\overset{\varphi}{\longrightarrow}\left\langle m\right\rangle\overset{\sigma}{\longrightarrow}\left\langle n\right\rangle$$to be the map
$$\sigma\circ\varphi:i\longmapsto\bigcup_{j\in\varphi\left(i\right)}\sigma\left(j\right).$$\end{defn}\par\begin{rem}The equivalence of categories between $\Gamma$ and $\mathsf{FinSet}_{\bullet}^{\mathsf{op}}$
is a $0$-truncated analogue of the Grothendieck construction - a
map of finite pointed sets is replaced with the data of the fibres
it parameterizes.
\end{rem}\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsubsection}{toc}{\label{subsec:The-Categorical-wreath}Berger's Categorical wreath
product}
\begin{defn}Let $A$ and $B$ be small categories. Given a functor $G:B\longrightarrow\Gamma$,
we define $B\int_{G}A=B\int A$ (with the second notation suppressing
the functor $G$ when the meaning is clear) be the category the objects
of which are
$$b;\left(a_{1},\dots,a_{m}\right)$$where $b$ is an object of $B$, $G\left(b\right)=\left\langle m\right\rangle$,
and $\left(a_{1},\dots,a_{m}\right)$ describes a function $G\left(b\right)\longrightarrow\mathsf{Ob}\left(A\right)$.
The morphisms of $B\int A$, denoted
$$g;\mathbf{f}:b;\left(a_{i}\right)_{i\in G\left(b\right)}\longrightarrow d;\left(c_{i}\right)_{i\in G\left(d\right)}$$are constituted of a morphism
$$g:b\longrightarrow d$$of $B$ and a morphism of $\widehat{A}$,
$$\mathbf{f}=\left(\left(f_{ji}:a_{i}\rightarrow c_{j}\right)_{j\in G\left(g\right)\left(i\right)}\right)_{i\in G\left(b\right)}:\prod_{i\in G\left(b\right)}\left(A^{a_{i}}\longrightarrow\prod_{j\in G\left(g\right)\left(i\right)}A^{c_{j}}\right)$$The composition
$$b;\left(a_{i}\right)_{i\in G\left(b\right)}\overset{g;\mathbf{f}}{\longrightarrow}d;\left(c_{i}\right)_{i\in G\left(d\right)}\overset{r;\mathbf{q}}{\longrightarrow}\ell;\left(k_{i}\right)_{i\in G\left(\ell\right)}$$is denoted $r\circ g;\mathbf{q}\circ\mathbf{f}$ where the meaning
of $r\circ g$ is clear and
$$\mathbf{q}\circ\mathbf{f}=\left(\left(q_{jk}\circ f_{ki}\right)_{j\in G\left(r\circ g\right)\left(i\right)}\right)_{i\in G\left(b\right)}$$with the varying values for $k\in G\left(d\right)$ in the expression
being those unique $k$ in $G\left(g\right)\left(i\right)$ such that
$j\in G\left(r\right)\left(k\right)$.
\end{defn}\par\begin{rem}We'll also use the more explicit notation $g;\left(f_{1},f_{2},\dots,f_{n}\right)$
where doing so simplifies the exposition.
\end{rem}\par\begin{example}\par Define the functor $F:\triangle\longrightarrow\Gamma$ by setting
$$F\left(\left[n\right]\right)=\left\langle n\right\rangle$$and setting for each $\varphi:\left[m\right]\longrightarrow\left[n\right]$,
$$F\left(\varphi\right):\left\langle m\right\rangle\longrightarrow\left\langle n\right\rangle$$to be the function
$$F\left(\varphi\right):\left\langle m\right\rangle\longrightarrow\mathsf{Sub}_{\mathsf{Set}}\left(\left\langle n\right\rangle\right)$$given thus:
$$F\left(\varphi\right)\left(i\right)=\left\{\left.j\right|\varphi\left(i-1\right)<j\leq\varphi\left(i\right)\right\}$$\end{example}\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsubsection}{toc}{The Categories $\Theta$ and $\Theta_{n}$}
\begin{defn}Let
$$\gamma:\triangle\longrightarrow\triangle\int\triangle$$be the obvious functor extending the assignment $\gamma\left(\left[n\right]\right)=\left[n\right];\left(\left[0\right],\dots,\left[0\right]\right)$.
We define the categories $\Theta_{n}$ to be the $n$-fold wreath
product of $\triangle$ with itself, i.e. we set
$$\Theta_{n}=\underbrace{\triangle\int\left(\cdots\int\triangle\right)}_{n-\mathrm{times}}$$We set $\Theta$ to be the conical colimit$$\underset{\longrightarrow}{\lim}\left\{\triangle\overset{\gamma}{\longrightarrow}\triangle\int\triangle\overset{\text{id}\int\gamma}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\right\}$$\end{defn}\par\begin{rem}It should also be noted that
$$\Theta\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\triangle\int\Theta\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\triangle\int\triangle\int\Theta\overset{\sim}{\rightarrow}\cdots$$so we may denote cells - where cells are the objects of $\Theta$
- in many compatible ways. For example for any $T$ a cell of $\Theta$
we may also write $T=\left[n\right];\left(T_{1},\dots,T_{n}\right)$
for some unique $n\in\mathbf{N}$ and unique $T_{1},\dots,T_{n}$ cells of
$\Theta$.
\end{rem}\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsubsection}{toc}{Reedy Structures on $\Theta_{n}$ and $\Theta$}
\par It is important to note that the categories $\Theta$ and $\Theta_{n}$
are Reedy categories. This fact was first proved in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Berger1}{}{}]}
and again by more general means in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{BergnerRezk}{}{}]}.
\begin{defn}Inductively, we define
$$\lambda_{\Theta_{n+1}}:\mathsf{Ob}\left(\Theta_{n+1}\right)\longrightarrow\mathbf{N}$$by setting
$$\lambda_{\Theta_{n+1}}\left(\left[m\right];\left(T_{1},\dots,T_{m}\right)\right)=m+\sum_{i=1}^{m}\lambda_{\Theta_{n}}\left(T_{i}\right)$$with $\lambda_{\Theta_{1}}=\lambda_{\triangle}$. The direct (wide) subcategories
$\Theta_{n}^{+},\Theta^{+}$ are subtended by the morphisms $f:S\longrightarrow T$
which pass to monomorphisms of presheaves under $\text{\char 72\relax}$. Similarly
the inverse (wide) subcategories $\Theta_{n}^{-},\Theta^{-}$ are
subtended by the morphisms of $\Theta_{n}$ (resp. $\Theta$) which
pass to epimorphisms under $\text{\char 72\relax}$.
\end{defn}\par\begin{lem}(Lemma 2.4(a) of \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Berger1}{}{}]}) The datum $\left(\Theta_{n},\Theta_{n}^{+},\Theta_{n}^{-},\lambda_{n}\right)$
(resp. $\left(\Theta,\Theta^{+},\Theta^{-},\lambda\right)$) comprises
a Reedy structure.
\end{lem}\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsubsection}{toc}{Inner horns and $\omega$-categories, outer horns and $\omega$-groupoids}
\begin{defn}Recall that a codimension 1 face $d:\left[m\right]\longrightarrow\left[m+1\right]$
of is {inner} if it preserves top and bottom elements and {outer}
if it does not. Inductively, we may then define {inner faces
of $\Theta_{n}$} as those maps
$$f;\left(\mathbf{g}\right):\left[m\right];\left(S_{1},\dots,S_{m}\right)\longrightarrow\left[n\right];\left(T_{1},\dots,T_{n}\right)$$for which:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$f$ is inner for $\triangle$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@and each component $g_{ji}:S_{i}\longrightarrow T_{j}$ of $\mathbf{g}$
is inner for $\Theta_{n-1}$.
\end{itemize}Given a face $\kappa:S\longrightarrow T$, let $\Lambda^{\kappa}\left[T\right]\subset\Theta\left[T\right]$
be the colimit
$$\underset{\Theta^{+}\downarrow T-\left\{\text{id},\kappa\right\}}{\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}}\Theta\left[S\right].$$Then the canonical inclusion $\Lambda^{\kappa}\left[T\right]\longrightarrow\Theta\left[T\right]$
is to be called an an {inner (resp. outer) horn of $\Theta\left[T\right]$}
if $\kappa$ is an inner (resp. outer) face of $T$. Let
$$\Lambda_{\mathsf{InnerBerger}}=\left\{\left.\Lambda^{\kappa}\left[T\right]\longrightarrow\Theta\left[T\right]\right|\kappa\in\mathsf{InnerFace}\left(T\right),\ T\in\mathsf{Ob}\left(\Theta\right)\right\}$$and let
$$\Lambda_{\mathsf{Berger}}=\left\{\left.\Lambda^{\kappa}\left[T\right]\longrightarrow\Theta\left[T\right]\right|\kappa\in\mathsf{Face}\left(T\right),\ T\in\mathsf{Ob}\left(\Theta\right)\right\}.$$\end{defn}\par\begin{prop}(\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Berger1}{}{}]}) An $n$-cellular
set (resp. cellular set) is the nerve of a strict $n$-category (resp.
$\omega$-category) if and only if it is right orthogonal to the regulus
$\Lambda_{\mathsf{InnerBerger}}$. Similarly, an $n$-cellular set
(resp. cellular set) is the nerve of a strict $n$-groupoid (resp.
strict $\omega$-groupoid) precisely if it is right orthogonal to
the regulus $\Lambda_{\mathsf{Berger}}$.
\end{prop}\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{$\protect\Z$-categories}
\begin{defn}Denote by $S$ the functor
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 13.65271pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.65271pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S:\mathbf{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.65271pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 73.65271pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{G}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise-6.76944pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{n}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.50002pt\raise-6.76944pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 75.67351pt\raise-6.76944pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 75.67351pt\raise-6.76944pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{n+1}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$This functor induces $S^{*}:\widehat{\mathbf{G}}\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{G}}$ which
``pulls down'' $n$-cells as $\left(n-1\right)$-cells and forgets
the preexisting $0$-cells. This operation preserves $T$-algebras;
$S^{*}$ induces a functor $S^{*}:\widehat{\mathbf{G}}^{T}\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{G}}^{T}$.
\end{defn}\par What then we ask is an object of the limit
$$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\rightarrow\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\right\}$$which we consider as the limit of the diagram $\mathbf{S}^{*}:\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$
which sends each $-n$ to $\widehat{\mathbf{G}}^{T}$ and each $-\left(n+1\right)\longrightarrow-n$
to $S^{*}$.
$$\cdots\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\widehat{\mathbf{G}}^{T}\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\widehat{\mathbf{G}}^{T}$$Now, Eilenberg-Moore objects for a monad $\left(T,\mu,\eta\right)$
in a $2$-category $A$ (here $\mathsf{CAT}$) are but lax-limits over the
diagram $\left\lceil T\right\rceil:\mathbf{2}\longrightarrow A$, which
classifies the underlying endo-functor. Then, since the taking of
limits commutes with the taking of limits, we find that there exists
some monad $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}T$ for which we have an isomorphism
$$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left(\widehat{\mathbf{G}}^{T}\right)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\widehat{\mathbf{G}}^{\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}T}$$More, we have an isomorphism
$$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\widehat{\mathbf{G}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\widehat{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}}$$with $\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}$ defined as follows.
\begin{defn}Let $\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}$ be the category
$$\left\langle\left.\vbox{\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 183.75018pt\raise-4.33163pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 183.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\cdots}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}\right|\begin{array}[]{c}s\circ t=s\circ s\\
t\circ t=t\circ s\end{array}\right\rangle$$\end{defn}\par Using the generalized spectrification of Theorem \ref{thm: generalized-spectrification-strict-limit}
we observe that the monad $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}T$, which we'll denote now by $T_{\mathbf{Z}}$,
is but $\mathsf{sp}\circ\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}T$ where $\mathsf{sp}$ is the left
adjoint to the inclusion
$$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\widehat{\mathbf{G}}^{T}\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\widehat{\mathbf{G}}^{T}\right\}\longrightarrow\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\left\{\cdots\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\widehat{\mathbf{G}}^{T}\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\widehat{\mathbf{G}}^{T}\right\}$$\par\begin{defn}Let the category $\widehat{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}}^{T_{\mathbf{Z}}}$ be called the $1$-category
of {strict-$\mathbf{Z}$-categories}.
\end{defn}\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{part}{toc}{Limits in the $2$-category of Categories with Arities}
\par In Proposition \ref{prop:Horn-filling-strict-shit} above we recalled
Berger's characterization of strict $\omega$-categories and strict
$\omega$-groupoids as cellular sets satisfying horn-filling conditions.
In this second part we develop theory enough to allow us to extend
this result to characterize $\mathbf{Z}$-categories as an orthogonal subcategory
of a category of $\mathbf{Z}$-cellular sets. We do this by developing the
theory of limits in Berger, Mellies and Weber's $2$-category with
arities and Bourke and Garner's synthesis of that work with Day's
density presentations.
\par\par\@@numbered@section{section}{toc}{Basic definitions: Variations on the $2$-category $\protect\CwA$ }
\par In \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{BergerMelliesWeber}{}{}]} we find the following $2$-category
defined.
\begin{defn}Let $\mathsf{CwA}$, the $2$-category of
{categories with arities,} be the $2$-category with:
\begin{description}\description@item@[CWA0]$0$-cells are locally small categories $\mathsf{E}$ together
with a dense, small, full subcategory $\mathsf{A}\overset{I}{\hookrightarrow}\mathsf{E}$,
denoted $\left(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{E},I\right)$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\description@item@[CWA1]$1$-cells $\left(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{E},I\right)\longrightarrow\left(\mathsf{B},\mathsf{F},J\right)$,

3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 90.30557pt\raise-54.47481pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise $\alpha:K\Rightarrow L:\left(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{E}\right)\rightarrow\left(\mathsf{B},\mathsf{F}\right)$
are (arbitrary) natural transformations $\alpha:K\Rightarrow L:\mathsf{E}\rightarrow\mathsf{F}$.
\end{description}\end{defn}\par\par Perhaps unsurprisingly we can, in a sense, switch the roles of $K$
and $\overline{K}$ in the definition of the $1$ and $2$-cells of
$\mathsf{CwA}$ - from $K$ as datum and $\overline{K}$ as part of a condition
to the reverse.
\begin{lem}Given categories with arities $\left(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{E},I\right)$ and $\left(\mathsf{B},\mathsf{F},J\right)$,
and a co-continuous functor $\overline{K}:\widehat{\mathsf{A}}\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathsf{B}}$,
if the square
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 12.22598pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.98611pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.92184pt\raise 5.89445pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.8889pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathscr{N}_{I}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.98611pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.22598pt\raise-21.83334pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{G}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-36.27779pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.98611pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widehat{\mathsf{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.7639pt\raise-21.83334pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.83888pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{K}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.7639pt\raise-36.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-5.84723pt\raise-43.66669pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{\mathsf{F}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.51039pt\raise-49.56113pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.8889pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathscr{N}_{J}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.98611pt\raise-43.66669pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 41.98611pt\raise-43.66669pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widehat{\mathsf{B}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$pseudo-commutes, then $\overline{K}=\mathsf{Lan}_{\text{\char 72\relax}}\left(\mathscr{N}_{J}\circ G\circ I\right)$
.
\end{lem}\par\@proof Since $\overline{K}$ is co-continuous and $\widehat{\mathsf{A}}$ is a presheaf
category, whence it is the free completion of $\mathsf{A}$ under colimits,
it follows that $\overline{K}=\mathsf{Lan}_{\text{\char 72\relax}}\left(K\right)$ for some
$K:\mathsf{A}\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathsf{B}}$. More, since
$$\mathsf{Lan}_{\text{\char 72\relax}}\left(K\right)=\mathsf{Lan}_{\mathscr{N}_{I}}\left(\mathsf{Lan}_{I}\left(K\right)\right)$$and the square pseudo-commutes by hypothesis we have
$$\mathscr{N}_{J}\circ G=\mathsf{Lan}_{\mathscr{N}_{I}}\left(\mathsf{Lan}_{I}\left(K\right)\right)\circ\mathscr{N}_{I}$$which, since $\mathscr{N}_{I}$ is full-and-faithful, provides that
$$\mathscr{N}_{J}\circ G=\mathsf{Lan}_{I}\left(K\right)$$which in turn provides, by pre-composition, the equality
$$\mathscr{N}_{J}\circ G\circ I=\mathsf{Lan}_{I}\left(K\right)\circ I$$and lastly, by the full-and-faithfulness of $I$, the equality
$$\mathscr{N}_{J}\circ G\circ I=K$$\qed\end@proof
In light of the lemma above, we provide an alternative description
of $\mathsf{CwA}$.
\begin{cor}The $2$-category defined by the axioms {CWA0 }from{
}in Definition \ref{def:BMW-definition-of-CwA} with the further axioms
\begin{description}\description@item@[CWA1$*$]$1$-cells $\left(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{E},I\right)\rightarrow\left(\mathsf{B},\mathsf{F},J\right)$
are co-continuous functors $\overline{K}:\widehat{\mathsf{A}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathsf{B}}$
which pseudo-factor as in
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 5.98611pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.98611pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern \begin{defn}A monad $T$ on $\mathsf{E}$ has arities $\mathsf{A}$ if the endofunctor $T$
is arities-respecting in the sense of {CWA1}.
\end{defn}\par\begin{rem}Those concerned with the fact that no criteria are imposed on the
$2$-cells of the monad $T$ may appeal to Lemma \ref{lem:Extension-of-monads}.
\end{rem}\par\begin{lem}Given a monad $T\circlearrowright\mathsf{E}$
and category with arities $\left(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{E},I\right)$, $T$ has arities
$\mathsf{A}$ precisely if, setting $\overline{T}=\mathsf{Lan}_{\mathscr{N}_{I}}\left(\mathscr{N}_{I}\circ T\right)$
, we have that $\widehat{\mathsf{A}}^{\overline{T}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\widehat{\mathsf{A}_{T}}$.
\end{lem}\par\@proof Suppose $T\circlearrowright\left(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{E},I\right)$. Since $\overline{T}$
is co-continuous $\widehat{\mathsf{A}}^{\overline{T}}=\widehat{\mathsf{A}_{\overline{T}}}$
and then, since $\mathscr{N}_{I}\circ T=\overline{T}\circ\mathscr{N}_{I}$, $\mathsf{A}_{\overline{T}}=\mathsf{A}_{T}$.
Conversely, suppose $\widehat{\mathsf{A}}^{\overline{T}}=\widehat{\mathsf{A}_{T}}$ with
$\overline{T}=\mathsf{Lan}_{\mathscr{N}_{I}}\left(\mathscr{N}_{I}\circ T\right)$. Since $\widehat{\mathsf{A}}^{\overline{T}}$
is a presheaf topos precisely if $\overline{T}$ is co-continuous
it follows that $\overline{T}=\mathsf{Lan}_{\text{\char 72\relax}_{\mathsf{A}}}\left(\overline{T}\circ\text{\char 72\relax}_{\mathsf{A}}\right)$.

\qed\end@proof
\begin{rem}Of the most useful and interesting aspects of the $2$-category $\mathsf{CwA}$
is that not only does it have Eilenberg-Moore objects for all monads,
but that the Eilenberg-Moore object of a monad is computable by way
of the bijective-on-objects full-and-faithful factorization system.
We will see this arising perhaps a bit less mysteriously from a limit
reflection result later on.
\end{rem}\par\begin{example}The monad $T$ on $\widehat{\mathbf{G}}$ whose algebras are strict-$\omega$-categories
is an arities preserving monad for $\mathbf{P}\longrightarrow\mathbf{G}$.
Moreover $\Theta\longrightarrow\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}$ is the Eilenberg-Moore object
for that monad in $\mathsf{CwA}$.
\end{example}\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{section}{toc}{From Arities to Sketches}
\par As described in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{BourkeGarner}{}{}]} the notion of density presentation,
due to Day, can be used to sharpen the abstract nerve theorm of Berger,
Mellies and Weber.
\begin{defn}A {set of colimits $\Psi$ in a category $\mathsf{C}$ }is a set
of diagrams
$$\left\{D_{j};I_{j}\longrightarrow\mathsf{C}\right\}_{j\in J}$$for whichthe colimit $\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}D_{i}$ exists for all $j\in J$.
\par Given a full-and-faithful functor $K:\mathsf{C}\longrightarrow\mathsf{A}$ and
a diagram $D:I\longrightarrow\mathsf{C}$ with colimit $\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}D$, we say
that the {colimit of $D$ is} {$K$-absolute }if the
nerve $\mathscr{N}_{K}:\mathsf{A}\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathsf{C}}$ preserves that colimit,
i.e. if the canonical comparison map $\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\mathscr{N}_{K}D\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathscr{N}_{K}\left(\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}D\right)$
is an isomorphism.
\par Given a set of colimits $\Psi$ in a category $\mathsf{C}$ and a full-and-faithful
functor $K:\mathsf{C}\longrightarrow\mathsf{A}$, if $\mathsf{A}$ is the closure of
$\mathsf{C}$ under a set of $K$-absolute colimits then $\Psi$ is said
to be a {density presentation} for $K$.
\end{defn}\par\begin{example}The set of wide spans

$X:\mathsf{A}_{M}^{\mathsf{op}}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Set}$ is the nerve
of an $M$-algbra if and only if $X$ is right orthogonal to the set
of monomorphisms
$$\mathsf{V}_{\Psi}=\left\{\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\mathscr{N}_{J_{M}}F^{M}JD_{\ell}\longrightarrow\mathscr{N}_{J_{M}}\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}F^{M}JD_{\ell}\right\}$$\end{prop}\par\@proof This follows from Theorem 36 of \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{BourkeGarner}{}{}]}.
\qed\end@proof
\begin{rem}In the case where the nerves of the colimits $\mathscr{N}_{J_{M}}\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}F^{M}D_{\ell}$
are representable by $T_{\ell}$ we will denote the colimit $\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\mathscr{N}_{J_{M}}F^{M}D_{\ell}$
by $V\left[T_{\ell}\right]$ for reasons which will become clear.
\end{rem}\par\begin{example}Recall that the set of wide spans
$$\Psi=\left\{\vbox{\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 124.00002pt\raise-4.70508pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 147.04208pt\raise-12.41945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5853pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{s^{n_{\ell}-m_{\ell-1}}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 171.0pt\raise-4.71045pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}\right\}$$constitute a density presentation for the inclusion $\mathbf{G}\longrightarrow\mathbf{P}$.
The set $\mathsf{V}_{\Psi}$ is the set of spine inclusions
$$\mathsf{V}=\left\{V\left[T\right]\longrightarrow\Theta\left[T\right]\right\}$$It follows then, from the proposition above, that a cellular set $X$
is the nerve of a strict $\omega$-category precisely if it is right
orthogonal to this set $\mathsf{V}$. Right orthogonality with respect
to the spines is of course the familiar Segal condition.
\end{example}\par We can use this proposition this to provide a more abstract proof
of Berger's cellular nerve criterion in terms of inner horns.
\begin{example}Let $\kappa:S\longrightarrow T$
be a hyperface of a cell $T$ of $\Theta$. We recall that $\Lambda^{\kappa}\left[T\right]$
is the union in $\Theta\left[T\right]$ of all hyperfaces of $T$
which are not the hyperface $\kappa$. Each presheaf $\Lambda^{\kappa}\left[T\right]$
is therefore a co-equalizer. Let $F_{\left(\kappa,T\right)}$ be the
set of hyperfaces of $T$ which are not the hyperface $\kappa$ and
let $I_{\left(\kappa,T\right)}$ be the set of representables including
into intersections if hyperfaces in $F_{\left(\kappa,T\right)}$.
\begin{rem}The strange wording regarding the definition of $I_{\left(\kappa,T\right)}$
here is chosen to accomodate the fact that hyperfaces in $\Theta$
may intersect as coproducts of representables and not always representables
as in the case of $\triangle$. Consider for example the intersection
$$\left[1\right];d^{0}\cap\left[1\right];d^{1}\longrightarrow\left[1\right];\left[1\right]$$which computes to the coproduct of representable presheaves $\Theta\left[0\right]\coprod\Theta\left[0\right]$.
\end{rem}\par Given any hyperface $\kappa:S\longrightarrow T$ of cell $T$ of $\Theta$
it follows that the canonical map
$$\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\prod_{R^{\mathcal{\prime}}\in I_{\left(\kappa,T\right)}}\Theta\left[R^{\mathcal{\prime}}\right]\rightrightarrows\prod_{R\in F_{\left(\kappa,T\right)}}\Theta\left[R\right]\right\}\longrightarrow\Theta\left[T\right]$$is an isomorphism.
\par The set of all of these pairs of parralel arrows comprise a density
presentation, call it $\Upsilon$, for the identity $\Theta\longrightarrow\Theta$.
We will use this density presentation to provide an alternative proof
of Berger's cellular nerve theorem as follows. Let $\overline{T}$
be the obvious left Kan extension of the monad $T\circlearrowright\widehat{\mathbf{G}}$
whose algebras are strict $\omega$-categories. Observe that $\Theta\longrightarrow\widehat{\Theta}^{\overline{T}}$
is the Eilenberg-Moore object for the arities preserving monad $\overline{T}$.
Thus Proposition \ref{prop:bourke-garner-density-pres-to-regulus-machine}
applies and a cellular set is the nerve of a strict $\omega$-category
precisely if it is right orthogonal to $\mathsf{V}_{\Upsilon}$ and the
set $\mathsf{V_{\Upsilon}}$ is the set $\Lambda_{\mathsf{inner}}$ by design.
\end{example}\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{section}{toc}{$\protect\Cat$-enriched Relative Adjunctions between $\protect\Cat\protect\hookrightarrow\protect\CAT$
and $\protect\Prof$, and $\protect\CwA$}
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{$\protect\Cat$-enriched relative adjunctions}
\par In this short subsection we recall the definition of $\mathsf{Cat}$-enriched
relative adjunctions and recall the associated limit and colimit preserving
properties of relative adjoint functors.
\begin{defn}Given a (not necessarily commuting) triangle of $2$-functors
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern natural equivalence of categories
$$\mathsf{C}\left(J\left(\_\right),D\left(\_\right)\right)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{D}\left(G\left(\_\right),\_\right)$$we say that {$G$ }is{ left adjoint to $D$ relative
to $J$.}
\end{defn}\par\begin{lem}Given {$G$ }left adjoint to $D$ relative to $J$ as in the
definition above:
\end{lem}\par\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$G$ preserves all weighted colimits that $J$ does; and
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$D$ preserves all weighted limits if $J$ is dense.
\end{itemize}\@proof Suppose $X:\mathsf{J}\longrightarrow\mathsf{A}$ to be a $2$-functor and $W:\mathsf{J}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Cat}$
to be a weight. Then, letting $\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}X$ denote the $W$-weighted
colimit of $X$, we see that if $J$ preserves $\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}X$ then,
for each $n\in\mathsf{B}$ we've the following sequence of equivalences
of categories.
\@@eqnarray
Since the equivalences are natural in $n$ and vary over all of $\mathsf{B}$,
this provides an isomorphism
$$G\left(\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}X\right)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}GX$$.
\par The similar argument for the weighted limit preservation arrives at
equivalences of categories
\@@eqnarray
which are natural in $a\in\mathsf{A}$. In the context of our assumption
that $J$ is dense, it follows that $D\left(\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}X\right)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}DX$.
\qed\end@proof
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{$\protect\Cat\protect\hookrightarrow\protect\CAT$-relative adjunctions}
\begin{lem}The functor $\mathsf{Prof}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$ is $\mathsf{Cat}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$-right
adjoint to $\mathsf{Cat}\rightarrow\mathsf{Prof}$.
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 12.5139pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.40279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{Cat}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.15488pt\raise-35.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-35.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-12.5139pt\raise-43.38889pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{CAT}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 48.5139pt\raise-43.38889pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{Prof}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.51392pt\raise-43.38889pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$\end{lem}\par\@proof See that we've a natural isomorphism of $\mathsf{Hom}$-categories
$$\mathsf{Prof}\left(\widehat{\mathsf{A}},\widehat{\mathsf{B}}\right)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{CAT}\left(\mathsf{A},\widehat{\mathsf{B}}\right)$$\qed\end@proof
\begin{lem}The functor
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 10.40279pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.40279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{Cat}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 56.4639pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 56.4639pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{CwA}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-6.33334pt\raise-7.66667pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.33334pt\raise-7.66667pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.40279pt\raise-7.66667pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 46.40279pt\raise-7.66667pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left(\mathsf{B},\mathsf{B},\text{id}_{\mathsf{B}}\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$is left $\mathsf{Cat}\hookrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$ adjoint to the functor
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 19.40973pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.94447pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{CwA}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 79.40973pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 79.40973pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{CAT}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-19.40973pt\raise-7.66667pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{E},I\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.40973pt\raise-7.66667pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 85.93752pt\raise-7.66667pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 85.93752pt\raise-7.66667pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{E}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$\end{lem}\par\@proof See that
$$\mathsf{CwA}\left(\left(\mathsf{B},\mathsf{B},\text{id}_{\mathsf{B}}\right),\left(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{E},I\right)\right)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{CAT}\left(\mathsf{B},\mathsf{E}\right)$$as the categories (left side) of pseudo-commuting squares (with right
vertical map co-continuous) and (right side) arrows are equivalent.
$$\left\{\vbox{\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 6.33334pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.33334pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 42.33334pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-36.27779pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 42.33334pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 48.11113pt\raise-21.86111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathsf{cc}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.11113pt\raise-36.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-5.98611pt\raise-43.72223pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 42.33334pt\raise-43.72223pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 6.33334pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.33334pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-35.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-5.98611pt\raise-43.44443pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{E}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}\right\}$$\qed\end@proof

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}$$\begin{rem}As consequences of these lemmata then, we find that:
\end{rem}\par\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$\mathsf{Cat}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Prof}$ and $\mathsf{Cat}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CwA}$ preserves
all colimits which the inclusion $\mathsf{Cat}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$ does;
and
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@since $\mathsf{Cat}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$ is dense, the functors $\mathsf{Prof}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$
and $\mathsf{CwA}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$ preserves all limits.
\end{itemize}\par\par\@@numbered@section{section}{toc}{A $3$-Oriental, a Comma Category, and the Limit Reflection of $\protect\CwA\protect\longrightarrow\protect\CAT^{\protect\bfTwo}$}
\par Now, it's pleasing to the eye to observe that the relative adjunctions
of the previous section assemble into a $3$-oriental suggestive of
a Yoneda structure. More, it's probable that some rumination upon
this will yield a formal theory of $\mathsf{CwA}$'s in an sufficiently endowed
$3$-category. However, our concern is more pedestrian.
\par The unit $2$-cell
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 12.5139pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr\cr&\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.40279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{Cat}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.5139pt\raise-18.3024pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-43.44441pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-25.44443pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.5139pt\raise-25.44443pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern 30.5139pt\raise-25.44443pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{Prof}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.41708pt\raise-43.44441pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-12.5139pt\raise-50.88885pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{CAT}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$induces a $2$-functor $\mathsf{CwA}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}\downarrow\left(\mathsf{Prof}\rightarrow\mathsf{CAT}\right)$,
and it's not hard to see that this $2$-functor is full-and-faithful
- consider our second definition for $\mathsf{CwA}$. Full-and-faithful functors
however reflect all (weighted) limits and colimits; by way of this
fact, and the limit preservation properties of Remark \ref{rem:limit-and-colimit-preservation-stuff-about-the-relative-adjunctions},
we develop a criterion for the existence of some weighted limits in
$\mathsf{CwA}$.
\begin{prop}The $2$-functor
$$\mathsf{CwA}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}^{\mathbf{2}}$$reflects all limits.
\end{prop}\par\@proof As we've already observed, the $2$-functor
$$\mathsf{CwA}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}\downarrow\left(\mathsf{Prof}\rightarrow\mathsf{CAT}\right)$$reflects limits as it is a full-sub-$2$-category. As such, we will
show that
$$\mathsf{CAT}\downarrow\left(\mathsf{Prof}\rightarrow\mathsf{CAT}\right)\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}^{\mathbf{2}}$$creates limits, whence the composition $\mathsf{CwA}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}^{\mathbf{2}}$
will reflect limits as well.
\par For the proof that $\mathsf{CAT}\downarrow\left(\mathsf{Prof}\rightarrow\mathsf{CAT}\right)\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}^{\mathbf{2}}$
creates limits we will apply Corollary \ref{cor:creativity-from-creativity}
and for that it suffices to show that $\mathsf{Prof}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$
creates limits. For the preservation part it is enough to recall that
$\mathsf{Prof}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$ is right adjoint relative to a dense functor.
For the reflectivity we observe that $\mathsf{Prof}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$ factors
as
$$\mathsf{Prof}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CoComp}\mathsf{CAT}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$$and the first leg is a full sub-$2$-category, whence it reflects
all limits, and
$$\mathsf{CoCompCAT}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$$creates all limits as it is monadic.
\qed\end@proof
\begin{cor}Let
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 25.44095pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-25.44095pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{E},I\right):J\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.22655pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 74.22655pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{CwA}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-5.34525pt\raise-7.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{j\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.34525pt\raise-7.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.44095pt\raise-7.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 61.44095pt\raise-7.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left(\mathsf{A}_{j},\mathsf{E}_{j},I_{j}\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$be a $2$-diagram and let $W:J\longrightarrow\mathsf{Cat}$ be a weight. Then
the weighted limit $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}\left(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{E},I\right)$ in $\mathsf{CwA}$
exists if:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@the weighted limit $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}\mathsf{A}$ exists in $\mathsf{Prof}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@the weighed limit $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}\mathscr{N}_{I}$ taken in $\mathsf{CAT}^{\mathbf{2}}$ is
induced by a full subcategory
$$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}\mathsf{A}\longrightarrow\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}\mathsf{E}$$\end{itemize}and in such case it is
$$\left(\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}_{\mathsf{Prof}}^{W}\mathsf{A},\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}_{\mathsf{CAT}}^{W}\mathsf{E},\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}\mathsf{A}\longrightarrow\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}\mathsf{E}\right)$$\end{cor}\par\@proof By the limit reflection proposition above it suffices to show that,
under the hypotheses of this corollary, the limit $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}\mathscr{N}_{I}$
lies in the image of $\mathsf{CwA}$. To that end, see that if the weighted
limit $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}\mathsf{A}$ exists in $\mathsf{Prof}$, $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}\mathscr{N}_{I}$ in
$\mathsf{CAT}^{\mathbf{2}}$ will be in the image of $\mathsf{CwA}$ if $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}\mathscr{N}_{I}$
is the nerve associated to a subcategory - density follows from the
fact that full-and-faithful functors are closed under limits, being,
as they are, a right orthogonality class.
\qed\end@proof
\par\par\@@numbered@section{section}{toc}{Examples}
\begin{example}{Eilenberg-Moore Objects of Monads with Arities }- Suppose
that $T\circlearrowright\left(\mathsf{A},\mathsf{E},I\right)$ is a monad in $\mathsf{CwA}$.
It follows from Corollary \ref{thm:Prof-is-the-obstruction} and Lemma
\ref{lem:monad-with-arities-and-presheaf-crap} that $\left(\mathsf{A}_{T},\mathsf{E}^{T},I^{T}\right)$
is the Eilenberg-Moore object for $T$ if $\mathsf{A}_{T}\longrightarrow\mathsf{E}^{T}$
is a full subcategory, but this much is clear.
\end{example}\par\begin{rem}The promised connection between this limit reflection result and the
bijective-on-objects full-and-faithful factorization system comes
from the fact that Kleisli objects in $\mathsf{Prof}$ are bijective on objects
functors $A\longrightarrow A_{T}$.
\end{rem}\par\begin{example}{A cellular
description of strict-$\mathbf{Z}$-categories - }In this example we will
identify useful arities for the category
$$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\xrightarrow{S^{*}}\mathsf{Str}\mathchar 45\relax\omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{Cat}\right\}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\widehat{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}}^{T_{\mathbf{Z}}}$$of $\mathbf{Z}$-categories.

60.26389pt\raise-56.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$We note that the columns of $\widehat{\mathbf{G}}$ 's and $\widehat{\mathbf{P}}$'s are comprised
of iso-fibrations, so the conical limits over them compute pseudo-limits.
The left-hand column of commuting squares induces a map
$$I_{\mathbf{Z}}:\underbrace{\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\leftarrow\mathbf{P}\leftarrow\mathbf{P}\right\}}_{\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{Z}}}\longrightarrow\underbrace{\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{G}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{G}}\right\}}_{\widehat{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}}}$$and the nerve associated to this map
$$\mathscr{N}_{I_{\mathbf{Z}}}:\widehat{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}}\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{Z}}}$$is the pseudo-limit in $\mathsf{CAT}^{\mathbf{2}}$ of the horizontal parts of
the right-hand column. By Theorem \ref{thm:Prof-is-the-obstruction}
to show that $\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{Z}},\widehat{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}},I_{\mathbb{\mathbf{Z}}}\right)$ is the
conical limit in $\mathsf{CwA}$ of
$$\cdots\rightarrow\left(\mathbf{P},\widehat{\mathbf{G}},I\right)\rightarrow\left(\mathbf{P},\widehat{\mathbf{G}},I\right)$$it then suffices to show that
$$I_{\mathbb{\mathbf{Z}}}:\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{Z}}\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}}$$is a full subcategory.
\par Using the $\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}^{\mathsf{op}}$ indexing for the copies of $\mathbf{P}$ in
$\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{Z}}$ and the $\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}$ indexing for the copies of $\widehat{\mathbf{G}}$
in $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\widehat{\mathbf{G}}$ we may write the map $I_{\mathbf{Z}}$ explicitly as
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 17.22774pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.93468pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{I_{\mathbf{Z}}:\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{Z}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 135.16394pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 135.16394pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{G}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{G}}\right\}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-17.22774pt\raise-8.38042pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left(-i,T\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.22775pt\raise-8.38042pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 77.22774pt\raise-8.38042pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 77.22774pt\raise-8.38042pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left(\left(S^{*}\right)^{i}\mathscr{N}_{I}\left(T\right),\dots,S^{*}\mathscr{N}_{I}\left(T\right),\mathscr{N}_{I}S_{\mathbf{P}}\left(T\right),\dots\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$Since $\mathscr{N}_{I}$ is a full subcategory, it follows that $I_{\mathbb{\mathbf{Z}}}$
is injective on objects and faithful, but since $S_{\mathbf{P}}$ is also
full-and-faithful, it follows that $I_{\mathbb{\mathbf{Z}}}$ is full as well. Composing
$I_{\mathbb{\mathbf{Z}}}$ with the equivalence $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\cdots\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{G}}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{G}}\right\}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\widehat{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}}$
permits to identify $I_{\mathbf{Z}}$ as the full subcategory of $\widehat{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}}$
on presheaves of the form
$$\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\vbox{\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&&&\cr&&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 171.0pt\raise-4.71045pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}\right\}$$for \emph{integers} $n_{0},m_{1},n_{1},\dots,n_{\ell-1},m_{\ell-1},n_{\ell}$
with each $m_{i}\leq n_{i-1},n_{i}$; $\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{Z}}$ is the full subcategory
of $\widehat{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}}$ of finite $\mathbf{Z}$-globular pasting diagrams. These
wide spans moreover comprise a density presentation $\Psi_{\mathbf{Z}}$ for
$\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{Z}}$.
\par Lastly, since limits commute with limits, whence limits over towers
commute with the taking of Eilenberg-Moore objects, it follows that
$$\left(\Theta_{\mathbb{\mathbf{Z}}}:=\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\dots\leftarrow\Theta\leftarrow\Theta\right\},\widehat{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}}^{T_{\mathbf{Z}}},I_{\mathbf{Z}}^{T}\right)$$is the Eilenberg-Moore object for $T_{\mathbf{Z}}$ as a monad in $\mathsf{CwA}$
on $\left(\mathbf{P}_{\mathbf{Z}},\widehat{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{Z}}},I_{\mathbf{Z}}\right)$. Thus, by Proposition
\ref{prop:bourke-garner-density-pres-to-regulus-machine}, and a repeat
of our argument from Example \ref{exa:Inner-horns-by-density-presentation}
it follows that a $\mathbf{Z}$-cellular set is the nerve of a strict-$\mathbf{Z}$-category
if and only if it is:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@right orthogonal to the $\mathbf{Z}$-cellular analog of the spines; or
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@right orthogonal to the $\mathbf{Z}$-cellular analog of the inner horns.
\end{itemize}\end{example}\par\begin{rem}In a sequel to this work, we will reprove a result of \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{LessardThesis}{}{}]},
and we will show how the homotopy coherent version of this cellular
presentation of $\mathbf{Z}$-categories provides a model of Spectra as pointed
$\mathbf{Z}$-groupoids.
\end{rem}\par\par\par\par\@@unnumbered@section{part}{Appendix}
\par\par\@@numbered@section{appendix}{toc}{\label{part:Generalized-Spectrification}\label{part:Generalized-Spectrification-1}Generalized
Spectrification}
\par Recall that in the $2$-category $\mathsf{Cat}$, given:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@a $2$-category $\mathsf{J}$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@a weight $2$-functor $W:\mathsf{J}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Cat}$; and
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@a $2$-diagram of categories $\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}:\mathsf{J}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Cat}$
\end{itemize}the $\mathsf{Hom}$-category of natural transformations and modifications
is determined by the $\mathsf{Hom}$-categories $\mathsf{Cat}\left(W_{i},\mathsf{X}_{j}\right)$
by way of the end formula
$$\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{Cat}\right]\left(W,\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}\right)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\int_{j\in\mathsf{J}}\mathsf{Cat}\left(W_{j},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{j}\right)$$\par We may then be wont to ask: which properties or structures involving
the values taken by the functor $\mathsf{Cat}\left(W_{\left(\_\right)},\mathsf{X}_{\left(\_\right)}\right)$
are sufficiently (and appropriately) coherent so that they induce
similar properties or structures on the weighted limit? In particular,
if for some objects $i$ and $j$ of $\mathsf{J}$, we have that $\mathsf{X}_{j}$
has all $W_{i}$ colimits then we have adjunctions
$$\left\{\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 19.68811pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.68811pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left[W_{i},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{j}\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.87785pt\raise 14.18947pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.68947pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{\left(i,j\right)}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 79.69412pt\raise 2.59633pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 79.68811pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{j}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.93912pt\raise-2.06944pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bot}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.59138pt\raise-14.56445pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.70335pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\triangle^{\left(i,j\right)}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 19.68689pt\raise-5.2046pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\right\}_{\left(i,j\right)\in\mathsf{Ob}\left(\mathsf{J}\right)^{2}}$$When do these object-wise adjunctions beget adjunctions in $\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{Cat}\right]$?
Taking a hint from classical treatments of stable homotopy theory
we develop an easy criterion and show, by explicit example, that the
spectrification functors of stable homotopy theory are recovered from
this $2$-categorical treatment.
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{A Criterion for small conical limits in $\protect\CAT$ to be reflective
in weighted-limits in $\protect\CAT$: generalized spectrification}
\begin{thm}Let $\mathsf{J}$
be a $1$-category and let $\mathsf{X}:\mathsf{J}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$ be
a diagram of categories. Suppose further that, for all $f:j\rightarrow k$
in $\mathsf{J}$:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@the diagram $\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}:\mathsf{J}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$ is fibrant in the injective-canonical
model structure on $\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{CAT}\right]$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@the category $\mathsf{X}_{k}$ admits all $W_{j}$ and $W_{k}$-colimits;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@the functor $\mathsf{X}_{f}$ preserves $W_{j}$ colimits; and
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@the functor $W_{f}$ is final.
\end{itemize}Then, the functor
$$\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 32.23618pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-32.23618pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{CAT}\right]\left(W,\mathsf{X}\right)}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 92.23618pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{CAT}\right]\left(\bullet,\mathsf{X}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.90979pt\raise 5.39168pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Delta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.23618pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$admits a left adjoint, which we will call $\mathsf{sp}$.\end{thm}\par\@proof Consider the commutative diagram
$$\vbox{\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 38.4584pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-38.4584pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{Ps}\left(\mathsf{J},\mathsf{CAT}\right)\left(W,\mathsf{X}\right)}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 74.4584pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{Ps}\left(\mathsf{J},\mathsf{CAT}\right)\left(\bullet,\mathsf{X}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 47.77957pt\raise 5.8978pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.8978pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Delta^{\mathcal{\prime}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.4584pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-32.23618pt\raise-44.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{CAT}\right]\left(W,\mathsf{X}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-17.85222pt\raise-22.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.8889pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{I_{W}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-8.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 80.68063pt\raise-44.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{CAT}\right]\left(\bullet,\mathsf{X}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 110.83348pt\raise-22.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{I}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 110.83348pt\raise-8.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.13202pt\raise-49.39166pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Delta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.23618pt\raise-44.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}$$where $I$ and $I_{W}$ are the obvious inclusions. Since $I_{W}$
is full-and-faithful, if $I_{W}\Delta$ admits a left adjoint functor
$L$, then $LI_{W}$ is left adjoint to $\triangle$. But $I_{W}\Delta=\Delta^{\mathcal{\prime}}I$,
so to produce a left adjoint to $\Delta$ it suffices to produce one
for $\Delta^{\mathcal{\prime}}I$. Since $\mathsf{X}$ is fibrant however, and $\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{CAT}\right]\left(\bullet,\mathsf{X}\right)$
enjoys the universal property of the conical limit $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\mathsf{X}$,
$I$ is an adjoint equivalence, whence to produce a left adjoint to
$\Delta^{\mathcal{\prime}}I$ it is enough to produce a left adjoint for $\Delta^{\mathcal{\prime}}$.
It is just such an adjoint we will now describe.
\par Recall that for any weight $W$, the $\mathsf{Hom}$-category $\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{CAT}\right]\left(W,\mathsf{X}\right)$,
may be computed as the equalizer
$$\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{CAT}\right]\left(W,\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}\right)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left\{\vbox{\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 29.98009pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-29.98009pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\prod_{i\in\mathsf{J}}\left[W_{i},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{i}\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 70.24362pt\raise 11.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rho}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 89.99194pt\raise 5.77228pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.94658pt\raise-11.43054pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\lambda}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 89.99194pt\raise-5.77228pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 89.98009pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\prod_{j\in\mathsf{J}}\prod_{k\in\mathsf{J}}\left[\mathsf{J}\left(j,k\right),\left[W_{j},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{k}\right]\right]}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}\right\}$$where
$$\frac{\rho_{j,k}:\left[W_{j},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{j}\right]\longrightarrow\left[\mathsf{J}\left(j,k\right),\left[W_{j},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{k}\right]\right]}{\left[W_{j},\mathsf{X}_{\left(\_\right)}\right]:\mathsf{J}\left(j,k\right)\longrightarrow\left[\left[W_{j},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{j}\right],\left[W_{j},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{k}\right]\right]}$$and like-wise
$$\frac{\lambda_{j,k}:\left[W_{k},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{k}\right]\longrightarrow\left[\mathsf{J}\left(j,k\right),\left[W_{j},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{k}\right]\right]}{\left[W_{\left(\_\right)},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{k}\right]:\mathsf{J}\left(j,k\right)\longrightarrow\left[\left[W_{k},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{k}\right],\left[W_{j},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{k}\right]\right]}$$More, since $\mathsf{J}$ is a $1$-category, we have an isomorphism
$$\prod_{j\in\mathsf{J}}\prod_{k\in\mathsf{J}}\left[\mathsf{J}\left(j,k\right),\left[W_{j},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{k}\right]\right]\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\prod_{j\in\mathsf{J}}\prod_{k\in\mathsf{J}}\prod_{f\in\mathsf{J}\left(j,k\right)}\left[W_{j},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{k}\right]$$and thus the map $\rho$ may be made explicit as a product.
$$\left(\left(\left[W_{j},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{f}\right]\right)_{f\in\mathsf{J}\left(j,k\right)}\right)_{k\in\mathsf{J}}:\prod_{j\in\mathsf{J}}\left(\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 20.13025pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-20.13025pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left[W_{j},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{j}\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 80.13025pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 80.13025pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underset{k\in\mathsf{J}}{\prod}\underset{f\in\mathsf{J}\left(j,k\right)}{\prod}\left[W_{j},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{k}\right]}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\right)$$Similarly $\lambda$ may be made explicit as the product.
$$\left(\left(\left[W_{f},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{k}\right]\right)_{f\in\mathsf{J}\left(j,k\right)}\right)_{j\in\mathsf{J}}:\prod_{k\in\mathsf{J}}\left(\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 20.83685pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-20.83685pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left[W_{k},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{k}\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 80.83685pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 80.83685pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\underset{j\in\mathsf{J}}{\prod}\underset{f\in\mathsf{J}\left(j,k\right)}{\prod}\left[W_{j},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{k}\right]}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\right)$$The pseudo-limit of that same diagram computes the $\mathsf{Hom}$-category
$\mathsf{Ps}\left(W,\mathsf{X}\right)$.
\par Thus, thinking of the taking of pseudo-equalizers as a functor
$$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{pslim}}:\mathsf{Ps}\left(\partial\overline{1},\mathsf{CAT}\right)\longrightarrow\mathsf{Cat}$$we see that $\Delta^{\mathcal{\prime}}$ is $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{pslim}}$ of the
pseudo-natural transformations of functors $\partial\overline{1}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$
which comprises the diagram

\par Our hypotheses will imply that the family
$$\left\{L^{\left(i,j\right)}=\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{\left(i,j\right)}:\left[W_{i},\mathsf{X}_{j}\right]\longrightarrow\mathsf{X}_{j}\right\}_{\left(i,j\right)\in\mathsf{Ob}\left(\mathsf{J}\right)^{2}}$$satisfies this criterion. Since $W_{f}$ is final for all morphisms
$f:j\rightarrow k$ of $\mathsf{J}$, for any $\phi\in\mathsf{Ob}\left(\left[W_{k},\mathsf{X}_{k}\right]\right)$
we have the following chase.

0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.87505pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\prod\left(\left(\mathsf{X}_{f}\right)\right)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 125.66338pt\raise-62.7827pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
