0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 125.67023pt\raise-67.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\prod_{j\in\mathsf{J}}\prod_{k\in\mathsf{J}}\prod_{f\in\mathsf{J}\left(j,k\right)}\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}_{k}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}$$pseudo-commutes. Then, defining functor $\mathsf{sp}^{\mathcal{\prime}}$ as the pseudo-limit
$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{pslim}}\left(\prod\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{\left(i,i\right)},\prod\prod\prod\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{\left(j,k\right)}\right)$,
we have that $\mathsf{sp^{\mathcal{\prime}}}$ is left adjoint to $\Delta^{\mathcal{\prime}}=\underleftarrow{\mathsf{pslim}}\left(\prod\Delta^{\left(i,i\right)},\prod\prod\prod\Delta^{\left(j,k\right)}\right)$.
In light of our earlier observations, we have $\mathsf{sp}=\mathsf{sp}^{\mathcal{\prime}}I_{W}$
is left adjoint to $\Delta$ and the theorem is proved.
\qed\end@proof
\par\begin{example}To make clear the necessity of the condition that $\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}$ is injective-canonical
fibrant, consider the following example. Let $\mathsf{J}=\mathbf{G}_{\leq 1}$ (the
ur-pair-of-parallel-arrow) and let $W=\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}$ be the $\mathsf{J}$-diagram
$$\bullet\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.20311pt\raise 10.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{s}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 87.00446pt\raise 1.27199pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 41.57985pt\raise-11.15277pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.15279pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 87.00446pt\raise-1.27199pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 87.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{I}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$where $\bullet$ is the terminal category and $\mathbf{I}$ is the
ur-isomorphism. Since $s$ and $t$ are equivalences of categories
and $\bullet$ is co-complete it follows that all hypotheses of the
theorem, save fibrancy, are satisfied. We see however that there does
not exist \emph{any }adjunction between $\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{CAT}\right]\left(\bullet,\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}\right)$
and $\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{CAT}\right]\left(W,\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}\right)$ as the former is the
empty category whereas the latter is a terminal category.
\par To see that the fibrancy hypothesis excludes this example we observe
that, since $\mathsf{J}$ is both direct and inverse, it follows that the
Reedy-canonical model structure on $\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{CAT}\right]$ ( picking
$\mathsf{J}^{-}=\mathsf{J}$ ) and the injective-canonical model structure coincide.
Thus $\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}$ is fibrant precisely if the matching maps
$$\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}\left(\overline{0}\right)\longrightarrow\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}\left(\overline{1}\right)\times\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}\left(\overline{1}\right)$$and
$$\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}\left(\overline{1}\right)\longrightarrow\bullet$$are iso-fibrations. While the second of those maps is an iso-fibration,
as all maps into the terminal category are iso-fibrations, the first
map, which evaluates to
$$\bullet\xrightarrow{\left(s,t\right)}\mathbf{I}\times\mathbf{I}$$is \emph{not }an iso-fibration as $\left(s,t\right)$, as an object
of $\mathbf{I}\times\mathbf{I}$, is isomorphic therein to all three
other objects of $\mathbf{I}\times\mathbf{I}$ and none of those isomorphisms
lift.
\end{example}\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{Examples: Spectrification Three Ways}
\par We now attend to the promised examples.
\begin{example}{Sequential spectra} - Let
$\mathsf{S}$ denote the usual category of spaces and let $\mathsf{S}_{\bullet}$
denote the category of pointed spaces. Let $\mathsf{J=\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}}$, let
$W=\left(\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}\downarrow\_\right)^{\mathsf{op}}:\mathsf{J}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Cat}$,
and let $\mathsf{X}:\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$ be the functor sending
each non-positive integer to $\mathsf{S}_{\bullet}$ and each $-\left(n+1\right)\longrightarrow n$
to $\Omega:\mathsf{S}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\mathsf{S}_{\bullet}$. As we will
show, these data satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem \ref{thm: generalized-spectrification-strict-limit}.
\par First, since $\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}$ is an inverse category, it follows that
the fibrant objects of the injective-canonical model structure are
but sequences of iso-fibrations - for any inverse category $A$ and
model category $C$, the fibrant objects are diagrams of fibrations
between fibrant objects and all categories are fibrant. Then since
$\Omega$ is an iso-fibration, it follows that $\mathsf{X}$ is injective-canonical fibrant. Second,
$\mathsf{S}_{\bullet}$ is co-complete so it has all $W_{-n}$-colimits.
Third, as $W_{-n}=\left(\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}\downarrow-n\right)^{\mathsf{op}}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbf{Z}_{\geq n}$
and $\mathbf{Z}_{\geq n+1}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Z}_{\geq n}$ is final, it follows
that $W$ is a diagram whose morphisms are all final functors. Lastly,
the functor $\Omega=\mathsf{Hom}\left(S^{1},\_\right)$ preserves sequential
colimits; given any $G:\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 0}\longrightarrow\mathsf{S}_{\bullet}$,
since any map $S^{1}\longrightarrow\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}G$ factors through some
$G\left(n\right)\longrightarrow\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}G$ it follows that $\mathsf{S}_{\bullet}\left(S^{1},\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}G\right)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\underrightarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\mathsf{S}_{\bullet}\left(S^{1},G\left(\_\right)\right)$.
Theorem \ref{thm: generalized-spectrification-strict-limit} then
provides that there is an adjunction
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 39.2356pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-39.2356pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\leq n},\mathsf{CAT}\right]\left(W,\mathsf{X}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 99.2356pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0},\mathsf{CAT}\right]\left(\bullet,\mathsf{X}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.82965pt\raise-2.06944pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bot}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.3991pt\raise-13.6111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\triangle}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{\hbox{\kern 39.21997pt\raise-6.15898pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$What we will now show is that this adjunction recovers $\Omega$-spectra
as a reflective subcategory of sequential spectra.
\par A sequential spectrum object in $\mathsf{S}_{\bullet}$ is comprised of:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@an $\mathbf{N}$ indexed family of pointed spaces $\left(X_{-n}\right)_{n\in\mathbf{N}}$;
together with
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@morphisms $\left(\phi_{-n}:X_{-n}\longrightarrow\Omega X_{-\left(n+1\right)}\right)_{n\in\mathbf{N}}$
where $\Omega=\mathsf{S}_{\bullet}\left(S^{1},\_\right)$, the usual loop-space
functor.
\end{itemize}The data of these objects is readily seen equivalent to that of oplax
cones over $\mathsf{X}:\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$, so the usual category
of sequential spectra is here recovered as $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{X}$. As we
will show, the weight $W$ is in fact the oplax-weight for $\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}$,
meaning that those oplax cones are equivalent to natural transformations
$$\Xi:W\Longrightarrow\mathsf{X}:\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$$\par Observe that the component of some natural transformation $\Xi$
$$\Xi:W\Longrightarrow\mathsf{X}:\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$$at the object $-n$ of $\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}$, is but a diagram
$$\cdots\longleftarrow X_{-\left(n+2\right)}^{-n}\longleftarrow X_{-\left(n+1\right)}^{-n}\longleftarrow X_{-n}^{-n}$$in $\mathsf{S}_{\bullet}$. Consider then that for each $f:-m\rightarrow-n$
of $\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}$, the naturality criterion
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern $$\left\{X_{-k}^{-n}=\Omega^{n-m}\left(X_{-k}^{-n}\right)\right\}_{-k\rightarrow-m\rightarrow-n\in\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}^{\mathbf{2}}}$$so our natural transformations are equivalent to oplax cones as follows:
the data of a natural transformation $\left(\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}\downarrow\_\right)^{\mathsf{op}}\Longrightarrow\mathsf{X}$
is given in the solid arrows and equalities in the diagram below,
and the data of an oplax cone is given by the objects along the diagonal
together with the dashed arrows.

157.73622pt\raise-32.92975pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$\par Now, taking a very strict definition, the category of sequential $\Omega$-spectra is the sub-category
of sequential spectra on spectrum objects for which the constituent
families of structure maps
$$\left(\phi_{-n}:X_{-n}\longrightarrow\Omega X_{-\left(n+1\right)}\right)_{n\in\mathbf{N}}$$are families of homeomorphisms. In terms of our functor $\mathsf{X}:\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}\longrightarrow\mathsf{CAT}$
this is but $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{pslim}}\mathsf{X}$ inside of $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\mathsf{X}$.
But $\mathsf{X}$ is injective-canonical fibrant, so $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\mathsf{X}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\underleftarrow{\mathsf{pslim}}\mathsf{X}$.
Now, it's not hard to see that the diagram

73.20839pt\raise-25.11388pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\Delta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.757pt\raise-19.72221pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$commutes, whence the left adjoint to $\Delta$ is the left adjoint
to the inclusion of sequential $\Omega$-spectra into sequential spectra,
which is to say that this left adjoint is spectrification.
\end{example}\par Lastly, this $2$-categorical treatment of spectrification also applies
to the more sophisticated notion of coordinate-free spectra found
in \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{ElmendorfKrizMandellMay}{}{}]}.
\begin{example}{Coordinate-free spectra} - keeping $\mathsf{S}$ and $\mathsf{S}_{\bullet}$
as above and fix some $\mathscr{U}\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{\mathbf{R}}^{\infty}$ (as vector spaces).
Let $\mathsf{FinSub}\left(\mathscr{U}\right)$ be the category of finite
dimensional sub-vector-spaces of $\mathscr{U}$. For each $V\in\mathsf{FinSub}\left(\mathscr{U}\right)$,
denote by $S^{V}$ the one-point compactification of $V$ and for
each $V\subset W\in\mathsf{FinSub}\left(\mathscr{U}\right)$, let $W-V$
denote the orthogonal complement of $V$ in $W$. A ($\mathscr{U}$
)-coordinate free spectrum object $X$ of $\mathsf{S_{\bullet}}$ is comprised
of:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@for each $V\in\mathsf{FinSub}\left(U\right)$, a pointed space $X_{V}$;
and
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@for each $V\longrightarrow W$ of $\mathsf{FinSub}\left(U\right)$, a
morphism $\sigma_{V,W}:X_{V}\longrightarrow\Omega^{W-V}\left(X_{W}\right)$
where $\Omega^{W-V}=\mathsf{S}_{\bullet}\left(S^{W-V},\_\right).$
\end{itemize}Denote the category of such by $\mathsf{CFSp}\left(\mathscr{U},\mathsf{S}_{\bullet},\Omega^{W-V}\right)$
\par A coordinate-free spectrum is, as in the previous example, equivalent
to an oplax cone over a $1$-diagram in $\mathsf{Cat}$ - here the diagram
is as follows.
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern W\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.11107pt\raise-15.90056pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 100.982pt\raise-15.90056pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Omega^{W-V}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$In the case of coordinate-free spectra, the meaning of $\Omega$-spectra
is essentially the same as with sequential spectra: a coordinate free
$\Omega$-spectrum has all is constituent morphisms $\sigma_{V,W}:X_{V}\longrightarrow\Omega^{W-V}\left(X_{W}\right)$
being homeomorphisms. We see again that
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left[\bullet,\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}\right]_{\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0},\mathsf{Cat}\right]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.2501pt\raise-26.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 72.00838pt\raise-20.57835pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\triangle}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 106.15976pt\raise-26.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 106.15976pt\raise-26.68945pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left[\left(\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0}\downarrow\_\right)^{\mathsf{op}},\mathfrak{\mathsf{X}}\right]_{\left[\mathbf{Z}_{\leq 0},\mathsf{Cat}\right]}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$commutes with the so labeled functors being equivalences of categories.
As before, by Theorem \ref{thm: generalized-spectrification-strict-limit},
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 46.15976pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-46.15976pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Omega\mathchar 45\relax\mathsf{CFSp}\left(\mathsf{U},\mathsf{S}_{\bullet},S^{\left(\_\right)}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.15977pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@hook{1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 106.15976pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 106.15976pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{CFSp}\left(\mathsf{U},\mathsf{S}_{\bullet},S^{\left(\_\right)}\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$will have a left adjoint as:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{\mathsf{CFS}}$ is injective-canonical fibrant
in $\left[\mathsf{FinSub}\left(\mathscr{U}\right)^{\mathsf{op}},\mathsf{CAT}\right]$;
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@Observe that picking $\mathsf{FinSub}\left(\mathscr{U}\right)^{\mathsf{op}}=\left(\mathsf{FinSub}\left(\mathscr{U}\right)^{\mathsf{op}}\right)^{-}$
defines a Reedy structure on $\mathsf{FinSub}\left(\mathscr{U}\right)^{\mathsf{op}}$
such that the injective-canonical and Reedy-canonical model structures
on $\left[\mathsf{FinSub}\left(\mathscr{U}\right)^{\mathsf{op}},\mathsf{CAT}\right]$
coincide. Thus to check fibrancy of $\boldsymbol{\Omega}_{\mathsf{CFS}}$
is to check that, for every $W\in\mathsf{FinSub}\left(\mathscr{U}\right)^{\mathsf{op}}$,
the map
$$\mathsf{S}_{\bullet}\longrightarrow\underset{W\hookleftarrow V:W\downarrow\mathsf{FinSub}\left(\mathscr{U}\right)^{\mathsf{op}}}{\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}}\mathsf{S}_{\bullet}$$is an iso-fibration. But since every square in $\mathsf{FinSub}\left(\mathscr{U}\right)^{\mathsf{op}}$
commutes, that limit is just the product
$$\prod_{V:\mathsf{codim}\mathchar 45\relax 1\left(W\right)}\mathsf{S_{\bullet}}$$over all co-dimension $1$ subspaces of $W$ and the map is the one
induced by that universal property by the family of maps $\left(\Omega\right)_{V:\mathsf{codim}\mathchar 45\relax 1\left(W\right)}$.
Indeed this is an iso-fibration as $\Omega$ is an iso-fibration.
\end{itemize}\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@the category $\mathsf{S}_{\bullet}$ admits all small colimits;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@the functors $\Omega^{W-V}$ preserves filtered colimits, whence $\left(\mathsf{FinSub}\left(\mathscr{U}\right)^{\mathsf{op}}\downarrow U\right)^{\mathsf{op}}$-colimits;
and
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@for any $V\subset U$ functor
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 44.56183pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-44.56183pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left(\mathsf{FinSub}\left(\mathscr{U}\right)^{\mathsf{op}}\downarrow U\right)^{\mathsf{op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 104.56183pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 104.56183pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left(\mathsf{FinSub}\left(\mathscr{U}\right)^{\mathsf{op}}\downarrow V\right)^{\mathsf{op}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$is final.
\end{itemize}\end{example}\par\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{appendix}{toc}{Oplax limits and The Oplax weight $\left(\protect\sfJ\downarrow\_\right)^{\protect\op}:\protect\sfJ\protect\longrightarrow\protect\Cat$}
\par The material of this section is well known to the $2$-categorical
cognoscenti.
We include it here for completeness, for notation, and for ease of
reference.
\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{Oplax natural transformations and the universal property of the oplax
limit}

0.76025pt\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}}}{\hbox{\kern-8.67853pt\raise-67.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y_{i}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.1178pt\raise-73.71109pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.40556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{Y_{g}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 69.72018pt\raise-67.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 69.72018pt\raise-67.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y_{j}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}$$We define $\text{$\mathsf{Oplax}\left(X,Y\right)$}$ to
be the $1$-category with:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@oplax-natural transformations $\boldsymbol{\alpha}:X\overset{\mathsf{oplax}}{\Longrightarrow}Y$
as $0$-cells; and
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@modifications $\mathbf{c}:\boldsymbol{\alpha}\Rrightarrow\boldsymbol{\beta}$
as $1$-cells.
\end{itemize}\end{defn}\par Having recalled the notion of oplax-natural transformation, we may
recall the notion of the oplax limit.
\begin{defn}For $2$-categories $\mathsf{J}$ and $\mathsf{D}$ and a $2$-functor $X:\mathsf{J}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}$,
then the oplax{ limit }of the $2$-diagram $X$, denoted
$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}X$ , is comprised of:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@an object $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}X$ of $\mathsf{D}$;
\end{itemize}and an oplax cone into $X$, i.e.
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@projection maps $\left\{\widetilde{\mathbf{\mathsf{pr}}_{i}}:\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}X\longrightarrow X_{i}\right\}_{i\in\mathsf{Ob}\left(\mathsf{J}\right)}$;
and
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$2$-cells $\left\{\pi_{f}:\widetilde{\mathbf{\mathsf{pr}}_{i}}\Longrightarrow f\circ\widetilde{\mathbf{\mathsf{pr}}_{j}}\right\}_{f:i\rightarrow j\in\text{$\mathsf{Mor}$}\left(\mathsf{J}\right)}$
\end{itemize}with:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@for all $i\overset{f}{\longrightarrow}j\overset{g}{\longrightarrow}k$
of $\mathsf{J}$, the diagram of $2$-cells commuting, i.e. $\left(g\pi_{f}\right)\circ\pi_{g}=\pi_{g\circ f}$
\end{itemize}enjoying the universal property:
\end{defn}\par\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@post-composition with the oplax-cone $\left(\left(\widetilde{\mathbf{\mathsf{pr}}_{i}}\right),\left(\pi_{f}\right)\right)$
induces an equivalence of categories.
$$\text{$\mathsf{\mathsf{D}}$}\left(A,\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}X\right)\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{Oplax}\left(\mathsf{J},\mathsf{C}\right)\left(\triangle A,X\right)$$\par\end{itemize}\begin{rem}It's worth noting that the suggestive (of the structure of a module)
criterion $\left(g\pi_{f}\right)\circ\pi_{g}=\pi_{g\circ f}$ in the
definition of an oplax cone can be born out (see \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Lack}{}{}]}).
\end{rem}\par\par\par\@@numbered@section{subsection}{toc}{\label{sec:Op-lax-limits-as-weighted-limits} The Oplax weight $\left(\protect\sfJ\downarrow\_\right)^{\protect\op}:\protect\sfJ\protect\longrightarrow\protect\Cat$}
\begin{defn}Given a $2$-category $\mathsf{J}$, call the $2$-functor
$$\left(\mathsf{J}\downarrow\_\right)^{\mathsf{op}}:\mathsf{J}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Cat}$$the {oplax weight}.
\end{defn}\par As one would hope, given the name, the oplax weight $\left(\mathsf{J}\downarrow\_\right)^{\mathsf{op}}$
computes oplax limits. See that, for a given $2$-functor $X:\mathsf{J}\longrightarrow\mathsf{Cat}$,
we've an oplax cone
$$\triangle\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{Cat}\right]\left(\left(\mathsf{J}\downarrow\_\right)^{\mathsf{op}},X\right)\overset{\mathsf{oplax}}{\Longrightarrow}X$$comprised of $1$-cells

\par What remains a bit opaque however is how, precisely, we translate
between oplax-natural transformations
$$\text{$\boldsymbol{\alpha}:X\overset{\mathsf{oplax}}{\Longrightarrow}Y$}$$and functors
$$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}:\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{Cat}\right]\left(\left(\mathsf{J}\downarrow\_\right)^{\mathsf{op}},X\right)\longrightarrow\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{Cat}\right]\left(\left(\mathsf{J}\downarrow\_\right)^{\mathsf{op}},Y\right)$$Every $\beta:\left(\mathsf{J}\downarrow\_\right)^{\mathsf{op}}\Longrightarrow X$
is comprised of a natural family of functors$$\left\{\beta^{j}:\left(\mathsf{J}\downarrow j\right)^{\mathsf{op}}\longrightarrow X_{j}\right\}_{j\in\mathsf{Ob}\left(\mathsf{J}\right)}$$We will describe $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}$ by describing its
action on the objects of $\left[\mathsf{J},\mathsf{Cat}\right]\left(\left(\mathsf{J}\downarrow\_\right)^{\mathsf{op}},X\right)$,
the natural transformations $\beta:\left(\mathsf{J}\downarrow\_\right)^{\mathsf{op}}\Longrightarrow X$,
component-wise. We set $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{oplaxlim}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}\left(\beta\right)$
to the natural transformation comprised of the family of functors
$\left\{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\left(\beta^{j}\right)\right\}_{j\in\mathsf{Ob}\left(\mathsf{J}\right)}$
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@whose actions on objects are given
$$\vbox{\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 32.28294pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-32.28294pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\left(\beta^{j}\right):\left(\mathsf{J}\downarrow j\right)^{\mathsf{op}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 96.62225pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 96.62225pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y_{j}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-18.98566pt\raise-8.38042pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left(\overset{q}{p\rightarrow j}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 18.98566pt\raise-8.38042pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@stopper}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.28294pt\raise-8.38042pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 68.28294pt\raise-8.38042pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y_{q}\circ\alpha_{p}\circ\beta^{p}\left(\text{id}_{p}\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}}$$and
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@which sends morphisms $\left(k\rightarrow j\right):\left(p\overset{q}{\rightarrow}j\right)\rightarrow\left(\ell\overset{q\circ k}{\longrightarrow}j\right)$
corresponding to triangles
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern \begin{lem}Given categories $\mathsf{A}$ and $\mathsf{B}$,
with $\mathsf{B}$ co-complete, and a functor $N:\mathsf{A}\longrightarrow\mathsf{B}$,
then the composition
$$\mathsf{Lan}_{N}\left(N\circ\_\right):\left[\mathsf{A},\mathsf{A}\right]\xrightarrow{\left[\mathsf{A},N\right]}\left[\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}\right]\xrightarrow{\mathsf{Lan}_{N}\left(\_\right)}\left[\mathsf{B},\mathsf{B}\right]$$is monoidal. If moreover:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$N$ is full-and-faithful and dense; and
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\mathsf{Lan}_{N}\left(N\circ\_\right):\left[\mathsf{A},\mathsf{A}\right]\longrightarrow\left[\mathsf{B},\mathsf{B}\right]_{\mathsf{cc}}\hookrightarrow\left[\mathsf{B},\mathsf{B}\right]$;
\end{itemize}then $\text{$\mathsf{Lan}$}_{N}\left(N\circ\_\right)$ is strong
monoidal.
\end{lem}\par\@proof The first part is well known. The second part is a weakening of a Math-overflow post by di-Liberti
(https://mathoverflow.net/questions/321909/extending-monads-along-dense-functors).
\par Since $N$ is dense if and only if $\mathsf{Lan}_{N}\left(N\right)=\text{id}_{\mathsf{A}}$,
thus for dense $N$, $\mathsf{Lan}_{N}\left(N\circ\_\right)$ strictly preserves
the unit. Since:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$N$ is full-and-faithful, $N\circ F=\mathsf{Lan}_{N}\left(N\circ F\right)\circ N$;
and
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@since $\mathsf{Lan}_{N}\left(N\circ F\right)$ is co-continuous for all $F:\mathsf{A}\rightarrow\mathsf{A}$,
$\mathsf{Lan}_{N}\left(N\circ F\right)$ preserves left Kan extensions, so
for any $F\circ G$ in $\left[\mathsf{A},\mathsf{A}\right]$ we have that $\mathsf{Lan}_{N}\left(N\circ F\right)\circ\mathsf{Lan}_{N}\left(N\circ G\right)=\mathsf{Lan}_{N}\left(\mathsf{Lan}_{N}\left(N\circ F\right)\circ N\circ G\right)$;
\end{itemize}thus
\@@eqnarray
\qed\end@proof
\par\par\@@numbered@section{appendix}{toc}{On Limits in Comma Categories}
\begin{lem}Let $\left(\mathsf{V},\otimes,\mathbf{1}\right)$
be a symmetric monoidal category and let $\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B},$ and $\mathsf{C}$
be $\mathsf{V}$-categories powered in $\mathsf{V}$. Then, given a comma category
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 6.33334pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 24.33334pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{F\downarrow G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.42549pt\raise-7.45834pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.875pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{\mathsf{pr}}_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.33334pt\raise-21.412pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 57.7465pt\raise-7.45834pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.875pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathbf{\mathsf{pr}}_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.57071pt\raise-21.41818pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-6.33334pt\raise-25.38889pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 74.57071pt\raise-25.38889pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1.5}\lx@xy@tip{-1.5}}}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.8711pt\raise-43.47499pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{L}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.25758pt\raise-46.89415pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 37.45203pt\raise-25.38889pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 74.57071pt\raise-25.38889pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 58.42856pt\raise-43.47499pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{R}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.64648pt\raise-46.88809pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-50.77777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 34.25758pt\raise-50.77777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{C}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$where $G$ is continuous and $\mathsf{C}$ is complete it follows that, if for some diagram
$$\left(a_{\bullet},f_{\bullet}:L\left(a_{\bullet}\right)\longrightarrow R\left(b_{\bullet}\right),b_{\bullet}\right):\mathsf{I}\longrightarrow L\downarrow R$$and some weight
$$W:\mathsf{I}\longrightarrow\mathsf{V}$$the weighted limits $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}a_{\bullet}$ and $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}b_{\bullet}$
exist in $\mathsf{A}$ and $\mathsf{B}$ respectively, then
$$\left(\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}a_{\bullet},\left(\widetilde{f}_{\bullet}\right),\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}b_{\bullet}\right)$$enjoys the universal property of the weighted limit $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}\left(a_{\bullet},f_{\bullet},b_{\bullet}\right)$
- with $\left(\widetilde{f}_{\bullet}\right)$ induced from the universal
property enjoyed
by $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}R\left(b_{\bullet}\right)=R\left(\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}^{W}b_{\bullet}\right)$
in $\mathsf{C}$.
\end{lem}\par\@proof We've assumed $\mathsf{A},\mathsf{B}$, and $\mathsf{C}$ to be $\mathsf{V}$-powered. It
therefore suffices to consider conical limits.
\par Now, the cone
$$L\left(\alpha\right):L\left(\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}a_{\bullet}\right)\Longrightarrow L\left(a_{\bullet}\right):\mathsf{I}\longrightarrow\mathsf{C}$$and the natural transformation
$$f_{\bullet}:L\left(a_{\bullet}\right)\Longrightarrow R\left(b_{\bullet}\right)$$compose to cone $\widetilde{f}_{\bullet}:=f_{\bullet}\circ\alpha:L\left(\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}a_{\bullet}\right)\Longrightarrow R\left(b_{\bullet}\right)$.
This cone induces, by the universal property of $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}R\left(b_{\bullet}\right)$
enjoyed by $R\left(\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}b_{\bullet}\right)$ as $R$ is continuous,
a morphism in $\mathsf{C}$
$$\left(\widetilde{f_{\bullet}}\right):L\left(\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}a_{\bullet}\right)\longrightarrow R\left(\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}b_{\bullet}\right)$$whence $\left(\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}a_{\bullet},\left(\widetilde{f_{\bullet}}\right),\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}b_{\bullet}\right)$
is an object of $L\downarrow R$. That $\left(\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}a_{\bullet},\left(\widetilde{f_{\bullet}}\right),\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}b_{\bullet}\right)$
enjoys the universal property of the limit in $L\downarrow R$ follows
immediately from the that of $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}a_{\bullet}$ and $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}b_{\bullet}$
in $\mathsf{A}$ and $\mathsf{B}$ respectively.
\qed\end@proof

\begin{description}\description@item@[Preservation]from the formula we have that, when $\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}b_{\bullet}$
exists in $\mathsf{B}$,
$$\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}\left(c_{\bullet},f_{\bullet}:c_{\bullet}\rightarrow R\left(b_{\bullet}\right),b_{\bullet}\right)=\left(\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}c_{\bullet},\left(\widetilde{f_{\bullet}}\right),\underleftarrow{\mathsf{lim}}b_{\bullet}\right)$$It is immediate then that $\mathsf{C}\downarrow R\longrightarrow\mathsf{C}\times\mathsf{B}$
preserves limits.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\description@item@[Reflection]since limits and colimits in products of categories
are computed factor-wise, if a cone
$$\left(c,f:c\rightarrow R\left(b\right),b\right)\Longrightarrow\left(c_{\bullet},f_{\bullet}:c_{\bullet}\rightarrow R\left(b_{\bullet}\right),b_{\bullet}\right)$$in $\mathsf{C}\downarrow R$ passes to a limiting cone
$$\left(c,b\right)\Longrightarrow\left(c_{\bullet},b_{\bullet}\right)$$in $\mathsf{C}\times\mathsf{B}$, then $c\Longrightarrow c_{\bullet}$ and $b\Longrightarrow b_{\bullet}$
are limiting. But then, since $R$ preserves limits, it follows from
the formula that $\left(c,f:c\rightarrow R\left(b\right),b\right)\Longrightarrow\left(c_{\bullet},f_{\bullet}:c_{\bullet}\rightarrow R\left(b_{\bullet}\right),b_{\bullet}\right)$
is limiting.
\end{description}\begin{cor}Let $\left(\mathsf{V},\otimes,\mathbf{1}\right)$

weighted limits.
\end{cor}\par\@proof See that the square
$$\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\immediate\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 13.91838pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-13.91838pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{C}\downarrow R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 49.91838pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-35.40219pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 10.18231pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces
\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-10.18231pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\line@@}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\line@@}}}\hbox{}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 49.91838pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{C}\times\mathsf{B}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 63.33507pt\raise-22.0211pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.90001pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\text{id}_{\mathsf{C}}\times R}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 63.33507pt\raise-36.59778pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-8.70972pt\raise-44.04222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{C}^{\mathbf{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.57446pt\raise-48.59776pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.55556pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathsf{res}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.05727pt\raise-44.04222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.05727pt\raise-44.04222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathsf{C}\times\mathsf{C}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces$$is a pullback. Since $\mathsf{C}\downarrow R\longrightarrow\mathsf{C}\times\mathsf{B}$
creates all limits by Corollary \ref{cor:creativity-from-continuity},
and $\mathsf{res}$ creates all limits, to prove $\mathsf{C}\downarrow R\longrightarrow\mathsf{C}^{\mathbf{2}}$
creates all limits, it suffices that $\text{id}_{\mathsf{C}}\times R$ creates
all limits, which it does as it creates the limits that $R$ does
- all of them.
\qed\end@proof
